<!-- lecture 1 -->

*A‘udhu billahi minash shaytanir rajim*. (I seek refuge in Allah from the accursed Satan.)
Please recite *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful.)
*Wa bihi nasta‘in, sallallahu ‘ala rasulihi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa ‘ala aali Sayyidina Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim*. (And in Him we seek help. May God's blessings, peace, and benedictions be upon His messenger, our master Muhammad, and upon the family of our master Muhammad.)

This is a book whose name is *Kunooz al-Hikmah al-madhkurah li-arbab al-‘ismah*. (The Stored Treasures of Wisdom for the Masters of Infallibility.)
Meaning, these are the treasures of wisdom that have been stored for *arbab al-‘ismah* (the masters of infallibility). Who are the *arbab al-‘ismah*? Those who possess *‘ismah* (infallibility). And they are the Ahl al-Bayt (`alayhimus salam) or their progeny. But here, that is not the intended meaning. Here, those who are the Ahl al-Bayt (`alayhimus salam) or the `A'immah Tahirin` (the Pure Imams), they are the masters of *kulli ‘ismah* (total infallibility). Meaning, it is impossible for them to make a mistake. They are free from sins. But the `du'at` (`alayhimus salam) are the masters of *juz’i ‘ismah* (partial infallibility). This has been clarified in a *risalah* (treatise) by Sayyidna Ali bin… Ali bin Hussain al-Jamari, who wrote a treatise in which he clarified that the `A'immah Tahirin` are the masters of *kulli ‘ismah*, but the `du'at` (`alayhimus salam), especially the `qa'imi du'at` (standing `du'at`), are masters of *juz’i ‘ismah*. And this is for them; that these *arbab al-‘ismah*, who are masters of *juz’i ‘ismah*, who are the `du'at` (`alayhimus salam), for them these matters have been stored so that they may derive new things from them.
This is a book by Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah, who is called al-Shamsi. He has a great station, in that Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib—he had several children, Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib did—he, Sayyidna Hibatullah bin Ibrahim. So, he performed *nass* (designation) on his eldest son. And he was his eldest child, Sayyidna Ali bin ‘Ibad. And Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah bin Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin... This is his lineage (`shajarah`). And this lineage continues all the way to Adam (`alayhis salam`), it reaches continuously. And then he passed away during his father's lifetime. So he became a `ghayr-munfarid Da'i` (a non-independent `Da'i`). And then his brother, on him `nass` was performed by Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib, who was eighth in the order of succession. When Sayyidna Ali bin ‘Ibad passed away, he was eighth in the order of succession. But Sayyidna Ali bin ‘Ibad performed *nass* on his other son, Sayyidna Isma'il, who was tenth in the order of succession. And it is said that Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah had the most excellent form (`surat`) in the community (`jama'at`) of `wulat` (`alayhimus salam). And another child of Sayyidna Hibatullah Sahib was Sayyidna Hasan bin ‘Ibad; after him, he also became a `Da'i`, a `Da'i` of great station. And another one was Sayyidna Ja'far, who was the 74th `Da'i`. So, he could not become a `Da'i`, but he has a great station, and he has written *qasidahs* (odes) and so on. So, Sayyidna Ja'far did.
(He also wrote a book?)
He also wrote a book... no, I don't remember. But the book *al-Kawakib al-Durriyah*, which is a book of great station, the *qasidah* at its beginning is by Sayyidna Ja'far, Sayyidna Ja'far bin Hibatullah.

And Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah wrote only one *risalah*, he wrote only one book, and did not write other books. He wrote it in his father's lifetime. Yes, he wrote it in his father's lifetime. And otherwise, your station is very high among the `du'at`.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful.) Above it is the *Ism al-A'zam* (The Greatest Name). It is said that in the *risalah* by Sayyidna al-Wajih—`Abd al-Ali bin Hasan al-Wajih` wrote a *risalah* in which he wrote great wonders and secrets, and the whole *risalah* is on the point of whether the *Ism al-A'zam* is greater or the *nur* (light) of the `Bismillah` is greater. He engaged in a lengthy discussion and he proved that the *nur* of the *Ism al-A'zam* is more excellent than that of *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. Therefore, when we write the `Bismillah`, we write the *Ism al-A'zam* above it. Yes. This is how it is. It's written.

(This has been found in some books, this...)
Yes.
(...the encoding of it.)
Yes, yes.
(It would have to be taken out.)
This is... This is *khatt al-Teseri* (Teseri script), isn't it? Yes, it's *Teseri*. So it would have to be looked at. This `Teseri`... that is, the first dot is a *khatam* (seal). It's a `khatam`. Meaning, it's a ring. And that ring alludes to the `Ahl al-Sharif` (the noble family). It alludes to the `Ahl al-Sharif`. And then the three... on this, there are also verses (`ash'ar`) by Amir al-Mu'minin, which I have, that you wrote on the `Ism al-A'zam`. And the `madd` (elongation mark) above it refers to the `mu'awanah` (assistance) that was received from the `Hudud 'Aliyah` (the higher ranks of the hierarchy); the `madd` points towards that. I don't remember the exact details, but God willing, if the occasion arises, I will bring them.
(So this thing is not written in this [book]?)
No, it's not. This is a separate thing.

*wal-hamdulillahirabbilalamin*. (And all praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds.)
*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabbil ‘alamin. Wal-‘aqibatu lil-muttaqin. Was-salatu was-salamu ‘ala Sayyidina Muhammadin wa alihi ajma‘in*. (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. All praise is for Allah, Lord of the worlds. And the best outcome is for the righteous. And blessings and peace be upon our master Muhammad and all his family.)
*Al-hamdu lillahil-‘azim jalla nasutun khas min riyadh ahlil-ikhtisas*. (All praise is for Allah, the Great, who manifested a special `nasut` (physical form) from the gardens of the people of specialty.)
Meaning, all praise and laudation are for Allah, that Exalted Being who created the special `nasut` from the `riyadh` (gardens) of the people of specialty. That is, the `riyadh` of the `ahl al-ikhtisas`... the types of `nasut` are several. There is `nasut adna` (the lowest `nasut`), `nasut awsat` (the middle `nasut`), and `nasut khas` (the special `nasut`). The `nasut tabi‘i` (natural `nasut`), which is the lowest, is also called `nasut tanasuli` (procreative `nasut`). And after that is the `nasut dhilli` (shadow `nasut`), the second one. And then after that is the `nasut khas`.
(The special one... who are these special people?)
`Ahl al-ikhtisas` means those who are the `hudud al-da'wah` (ranks of the `da'wah`) in the `jaza'ir` (regions), and the `mu'minin` (believers) and `hadarat` (dignitaries) there. It is made from them.

*Wa ja‘ala al-nasut al-dani min riyadh al-madunun fil-maqam*. (And He made the lower `nasut` from the gardens of those who are lesser in rank.)
And the `nasut dani` (lower `nasut`) is made from the `riyadh` of those who are distant from them, who are lesser than them in rank. Yes.
(He is talking about life (`zindagi`), right?)
He is talking about `nasut`... life... that is, `nasut`... `min riyadh`... *nasut dani*. It is mentioned here, right? This refers to the `nasut dhilli` or the `nasut tabi'i`.

*Ahmaduhu hamda man abda al-ma‘na al-zahir*. (I praise Him with the praise of one who has manifested the apparent meaning.)
I praise Him with such a praise... with the worship of one whose meaning is luminous. *Wa ‘arafa haqqa al-ism al-zahir*. (And he knew the reality of the apparent name.) And he knew the reality of this apparent name. Meaning, I grasped the apparent (`zahiri`) reality of the name as well as the inner (`batini`) meaning.
*Wa ashhadu an la ilaha illa alladhi tasawa fil-‘ajzi ‘an idraki huwiyyatihi al-muta‘aliyah awwalu mawjudin wa akhiruhu min bariyyatihi*. (And I bear witness that there is no god but He, in the inability to comprehend whose exalted essence the first of existing things and the last of His creation are equal.)
And I bear witness that there is no deity, that is, no one worthy of worship, but He, that the first existent thing and the last existent thing in His creation are equal in the inability to comprehend His exalted essence. That is, the first existent thing, who is the `awwal mawjud`? The First Intellect (`'aql-e-awwal`). The `awwal mawjud` and `akhir mawjud` (the last existent thing), from his creation, are equal. That is, the `awwal mawjud`, who is the First Intellect, whose station is lofty, and the last existent thing—who is the last existent thing? The believer. That believer who has entered the `da'irah-e-iman` (circle of faith). He. Both of them are equal in the inability to have gnosis of God. The First Intellect has not increased in gnosis, [in relation to] God, and this last existent thing has not decreased in gnosis of the First Intellect. Rather, his gnosis is not possible.
(This is about the believer, not for all humanity.)
No, no. The last existent thing. It means that existing things... what does "existent thing" (`mawjud`) mean? A "thing" (`shay'`). "Thing" refers to that which is in reality, that... *inna Allaha ‘ala kulli shay'in qadir*. (Indeed, Allah has power over all things.) That Allah is powerful over every "thing". So "thing" here refers to the believer, whose value exists. `La shay'` ("no-thing") is that which is a mirage, whose value does not exist; it appears to be water, but when you reach it, it is not water. So it has no value. For example, it has come in three places in the Holy Quran: *inna Allaha ‘ala kulli shay'in qadir*. (Indeed, Allah has power over all things.) That Allah has power over everything, meaning Allah has power over every believer, especially. And then, *inna Allaha bikulli shay'in ‘alim*. (Indeed, Allah is knowledgeable of all things.) He is aware of everything. And *inna Allaha bikulli shay'in muhit*. (Indeed, Allah encompasses all things.) He has encompassed everything. So these three things have come in reference to the believer. That is... that means the Imam of the time.

And this state, that both are equal in inability, the First Intellect and the last existent thing. So, it is said that when people used to come to meet Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman, you would stand up. You would stand up to receive them. And we have also seen the `du'at` that they would remain standing while they would shake hands for `juma'` (Friday) and other handshakes. So, you were asked, "Why do you stand up? You please be seated, Mawlana." So you said, "No, I will remain standing." Then later you clarified that "I also stand up to shake a believer's hand because he is my companion in the `Ahl al-Sharif`." In the *tawzi'`* of the `Ahl al-Sharif`, he... just as an Imam has taken his share in the `Ahl al-Sharif`, and a believer is also taking it, so in the `Ahl al-Sharif`, he is my companion. Therefore, I, giving him honor, stand up to... And then you see that Sayyidna Hussain bin Isma'il, may his holy secret be sanctified, what he wrote about Sayyidna Abdullah ibn Muhammad and Sayyidna Ahmad al-Jamari—what did he do there? That, `afdal al-mawjudat` (the most excellent of existing things). Abdullah ibn Muhammad is `afdal al-mawjudat`. So people there started to think, "What did the `Da'i` say?" `Afdal al-mawjudat` means he who is present in the `da'wah`. The believers. Among the believers, he is the most excellent. He pointed this out.

*Wa ashhadu anna Muhammadan ‘abduhu wa rasuluh*. (And I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and His messenger.) And I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and His messenger. *Al-murtaqi min nasut al-khas ila jawar dhi al-‘izzah wal-jabarut*. (The one who ascended from the special `nasut` to the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power.) That you ascended from the special `nasut` to the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power. Meaning, the Mi'raj (ascension) you had, in that Mi'raj, through which `nasut` did you go? It was through the special `nasut`, which was made from the `riyadh` (gardens) of the `ahl al-ikhtisas` (people of specialty). Who are the `ahl al-ikhtisas`? The `hudud` (ranks) of the `da'wah`, or the `mu'minin` (believers) and the `hudud` of the `hadarat` (dignitaries) there, and from the `riyadh` of the `hudud` of the regions, it was made. From those two `riyadh`, the `nasut` was made, the `nasut khas` (special `nasut`). From that, you ascended and were exalted in the proximity of the Possessor of Might and Power.
(`Jabarut` means?)
`Jabarut` means the powerful one. From `jabr`. Yes.

*Sallallahu ‘alayhi wa ‘ala akhihi*. (May God's blessings be upon him and upon his brother.) May God's blessings be upon you. *Al-mustakhlaf ‘ala al-ummah al-Muhammadiyyah talbisan li-sur al-radiyyah*. ([And upon his brother], the appointed successor over the Muhammadan community, clothing himself in lowly forms.) That he is the one who became the successor over the Muhammadan community. When the Prophet passed away, so... that is, before passing away, when at Ghadir Khumm he declared, "`man kuntu mawlahu fa-hadha ‘Aliyyun mawlahu`" (Of whomsoever I am master, this Ali is his master), so at that time, the remaining life that the Prophet lived, he lived it in the `hijab` (veil) of Ali. And Ali lived his life in the `hijab` of the Prophet. This is what it points to.
(So whose death occurred?)
Whose death occurred? It is said that Sayyidna Isma'il bin Ibrahim has stated that he did not clarify it, but he said that the `awsiya` (successors) pass away before the `natiq` (speaker-prophet). He has written this somewhere. That the `awsiya` pass away before the `natiq`.

So, the remaining life of Mawla Ali, he was the Prophet. He was in the Prophet's `hijab`. (`lahmi lahmuka wa dami damuka`) (My flesh is your flesh and my blood is your blood). And this is from `ruh wahidah` (a single soul), it was declared.
(And when the Prophet's body was present, he was the Prophet.)
Yes. And when the realities were manifest, it was that he was in the Prophet's `hijab`.

(In the battle of Jamal, Ali said, "I divorce you.")
Yes, absolutely. That you said, that to Lady A'isha, when she was preparing for war, that "I give Ali the right that after my passing, he may divorce you." And this matter became common knowledge.
(How is that possible?)
How is that possible? And the proof is from the Holy Quran, that `ta‘alaw abna'ana wa abna'akum wa nisa'ana wa nisa'akum wa anfusana wa anfusakum`. (Come, let us call our sons and your sons, our women and your women, our selves and your selves.) That "you bring your children." So "our children" were... you took Hasan and Hussain. And `anfusana wa anfusakum`, "you bring your selves, we will bring our selves." So in "our selves," who? You took Sayyidna Ali. So he became the `nafs` (self) of the Prophet.

*al-muhtajib bihi al-‘ashir bil-amr al-nahir*. (The Tenth who is veiled by him in the manifest affair.) That he was veiled... `al-‘ashir` (the Tenth). From this `al-‘ashir`, who is meant? That is, the one who was `al-‘ashir`, he became veiled. `Al-‘ashir` was not... `al-‘ashir` changed into another rank. The first `al-‘ashir` did not remain. So who became `al-‘ashir`? The one who, after him, was in the subsequent series, the lofty intellect, within its sphere, he became `al-‘ashir`. And that, he who is this `sahib al-qawr` (master of the cycle), from this `sahib al-qawr`, that `juz'-e-idafiyyah` (relative part) of this `qawr` (cycle), that is what is meant here. Abdullah Sahib always used to say that `al-‘ashir` here does not mean that `al-‘ashir`, but rather the `juz'-e-idafiyyah` of this `qawr`. That `Da'i` who is the `sahib al-qawr`, not the `sahib al-qawr`, but the `juz'-e-idafiyyah`, the `juz'-e-idafiyyah` of this `qawr`, he became veiled by you.

*Wa ‘ala fatin mudawij al-a'immah*. (And upon the youth who is the spouse of the Imams.) And upon the `fatin` (youth), who became the spouse of the Imams. That is, she became a wife, and... that is, she became the wife, and she became the spouse of the Imams from... that is, she was the wife, and... she was the wife of Ali, so from him... she became the spouse of the Imams from Ali.
*bi-sirrin daqqa ma‘nahu*. (with a secret whose meaning is subtle.) With such a secret that its meaning is very profound. That is, as the Prophet said in the presence of the Commander of the Faithful and in... *khalifatu abiha*. (The successor of her father.) That "she is my father's successor..." ...and `hujjatu ba‘liha`. (The `hujjah` of her husband.) "She is the `hujjah` of her husband." And `afdal al-hujjah` (the most excellent `hujjah`) is you yourself. Because of which, the Shahr al-Rajab al-Murajjab, which is attributed to Ali (`alayhis salam`), its first night is also of great station. And the first night alludes to Lady Fatimah, who is your `hujjat al-uzma` (greatest `hujjah`).
(In that era, did the `du'at` have knowledge of these matters?)
Yes, absolutely.
(So the caliphs would not have known?)
Some things were known. In the `haqa'iq` (realities), some things were known, but not entirely. Abu Bakr and Umar knew some things because they were from the time of `Abd al-Muttalib`, so some things were known. But Umar... there is a famous incident with Umar too, that he said to Hasan or Hussain, "These hairs were not grown by you." That is, "The hairs on your head were not grown by me, but by you and your father." And there is a tradition among the common folk too.
(So this means they knew about their high rank.)
Yes. And one time, he had gone and sat on the `minbar` (pulpit), Imam Hasan or Hussain. And he said that "this `minbar` belongs to my father." "It belongs to my father." So Umar testified, "Yes, this `minbar` belongs to your father." And then he sat down below.

So, the successor who was appointed for the community of the Prophet was Lady Fatimah al-Zahra. And because of this, your passing occurred... first of all, you passed away within six months. And when you would arrive, the Prophet would stand up. *Wa ajlasaha fi maqamiha*. (...and he would seat her in his place.) Or *fi maqamihi*. (...in his place.) You would seat her in your own place. This was a great thing. That is, this was to indicate that the one who will sit in my place is Lady Fatimah al-Zahra.

*Wa ghaba ‘an hadha al-sirr illa ahluhu wa ahwah*. (And this secret was hidden from all but its people and its folk.) And those who were unaware of this secret, of its secrets, its hidden matters, were unaware of it, except for those who were its people.
*Wa ‘ala al-Hasan al-mustawda‘ sirr al-imamah*. (And upon al-Hasan, the trustee of the secret of the Imamate.) That Hasan is such that he is the trustee of the secret of the Imamate.
*Wal-qa'im min al-nasut ila al-lahut ka-arbab al-za‘amah*. (And the one who rises from `nasut` to `lahut` like the masters of leadership.) And he is the one who rises from `nasut` to `lahut`, just as the `arbab al-za'amah` (masters of leadership), the noble ones, like them, he rises from `nasut` to `lahut`. Meaning, even while designated as the trustee (`mustawda'`), he has a `nasut` and a `lahut`. This is the meaning.
(All the `du'at` are connected to Hasan.)
Yes. All the `du'at` are connected to Hasan.
(The `A'immah` are connected to Hussain.)
Yes, the `A'immah` are connected to Sayyidna Hussain. But all the `du'at` are connected to Imam Hasan. And in the Holy Quran, the Surah al-Nas is what alludes to Sayyidna Hasan. *Qul a‘udhu birabbin-nas*. (Say, "I seek refuge in the Lord of mankind.") That "I take refuge with the Lord of mankind." `Nas` here refers to those who are *manusina bid-da'wah* (intimate with the `da'wah`), who have affection for the `da'wah`. Their Lord. So who became the Lord of mankind? That is Sayyidna Hasan. And *rabb al-falaq*. (The Lord of the dawn/daybreak.) *Qul a‘udhu birabbil-falaq*. (Say, "I seek refuge in the Lord of the dawn.") "I take refuge with the Lord of the dawn." So, `falaq` means to split. So, splitting... what is meant by splitting? From there, the Imamate began.

*Wa ‘ala al-Hussain al-muzhar al-ghaybah*. (And upon al-Hussain, the one who made manifest the occultation.) And upon Hussain too, may blessings and peace be, who is the one who made manifest the occultation (`ghaybah`). That is, he made his own self absent. *bil-qatli lada ahl al-raybah*. (through death at the hands of the people of doubt.) The people of doubt, those who had doubt, in front of them, your murder... that you were killed. Can an Imam be killed? He is so infallible. So he is the one who made death manifest. He manifested his own death.

*Wa ‘alal a'immati min dhurriyyatihi majami' al-anwar*. (And upon the Imams from his progeny, the assemblages of lights.) And upon the Imams from his progeny... who are the assemblages of lights. `Majami' al-anwar` (assemblages of lights) means what? `Anwar` (lights) and `asrar` (secrets) are two different things. `Asrar` is used for `niyyat` (intention). And `anwar`, as Sayyidna Sulayman... he has written in this very book that he is the `jami' al-asrar` (assembler of secrets) of the `du'at` of Yemen, who became `du'at` after Sayyidna Dhu'ayb bin Musa; he is the assembler of their secrets. He has written this. So there, `asrar`... the word `asrar` is used there. `Anwar` is not used. And `anwar` refers to the `suwarat` (forms). The luminous forms, he is their assembler.
*Wa asma' al-‘ayn al-‘azimah fil-‘ali min al-asrar*. (And the names of the great essence in the high secrets.) And the names of the great essence... they are the names of, who? These `A'immah Tahirin` are the names of the great essence, who are in the great, high secrets. Because the great essence, all the `A'immah` who came, until the Day of Resurrection, they are veiled by him. And he is their `hijab` (veil). These Imams are a `hijab`. And their veiled one is Amir al-Mu'minin.

*Wa ‘ala Imam al-zaman al-mutawari bil-hijabi*. (And upon the Imam of the Time, who is concealed by the veil.) *Al-mustatir bil-sirr ‘an jami' al-bara'a*. (The one who is hidden by the secret from all of creation.) And he is such that he is hidden by a great secret from all of creation. Your original self is hidden, and what has appeared is a `hijab` that has appeared.
*Wa sallama ‘alayhim wa ‘ala abwabihim al-mutalali' anwaruhum fis-suwar*. (And may peace be upon them and upon their `abwab` (gates), whose lights shine forth in the forms.)
*Wa ‘ala hujajihim wa ‘ala du'at hadha al-dawr al-maymun al-qa'in khamsina alfa sanah mimma ta‘uddun*. (And upon their `hujjahs`, and upon the `du'at` of this auspicious `dawr` (era), which will last for fifty thousand years of what you count.)
Now look, what did he say? That "upon the `du'at` of this `dawr` (era)"—that is, this `dawr` of the `qa'imi` (the era of the Qa'im)—"upon its `du'at` too, may God's peace be upon them." Who is this, that this `dawr` will last for 50,000 years. Where did 50,000 come from? That is, when the Qa'im will come, and from after him, 50,000 years will begin. So you have included this `dawr` in it. That is, the `dawr al-satr` (era of concealment) ended with Sayyidna Sulayman Sahib. The seven eras finished. According to calculation as well, seven eras are finished. And this `dawr` that is, it is included in the `dawr` of the Qa'im. That's why he says, *al-qa'in khamsina alfa sanah mimma ta‘uddun*.
*Wa Sulayman bin Hasan al-mujtama' ladayhi asraru du'at al-Yaman*. (And Sulayman bin Hasan, with whom are gathered the secrets of the `du'at` of Yemen.) That the secrets were gathered in whose possession? With you. So what a great station is yours!
*Wa Ja‘far al-sabi' fis-sirr al-ma'mun*. (And Ja'far, the seventh in the protected secret.) Ja'far, who is the seventh, that is, being the 28th, you became the seventh. In the manifest, protected secret.
*Wa mimun min la'la*. (And a Mim from...) Mim, who? Mim, Muhammad bin Fu'ad. That intermediate one who came... although he was non-independent, but he came in between.
*Al-qa'im bi-fard al-jihad fi mala'in*. (The one who established the duty of jihad amongst the assembly.) He is such that he is the one who establishes the duty of jihad in the middle of the assembly, that is, among the people. You established jihad. What a tremendous jihad you carried out! Imagine that if only Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fu'ad's position had been ruined, as happened with `Abd al-Muttalib` and others, then the `da'wah`... what would have become of it? The history of the `da'wah` would have changed. But what a tremendous jihad you carried out!
*Wa al-‘ayn al-mutafajjir ‘ala lisanihi yanabi‘ al-asrar*. (And 'Ayn, upon whose tongue gushed forth the springs of secrets.) And `Ayn`—`Ayn` means Sayyidna Ali bin Sulayman—such that gushing forth from his tongue are the springs of secrets. The various secrets of the doctrines, their springs, their fountains, are manifest from your tongue. And they were, that in your `haqa'iq` and other books, what sublime matters of doctrine you have presented, which had not happened before. You have manifested them. And *Shu'al al-Anwar*, which is the first book, you yourself have written that "this *Shu'al al-Anwar* is in the `haqa'iq` (realities) and the `daqa'iq` (subtleties)." You have said this. *hadha al-kitab fil haqa'iq wad-daqa'iq*. (This book is on the realities and the subtleties.) From there, the books of subtleties began.
(Syedna Ja'far did not give it on his own.)
No. And Syedna Ja'far did not write a single book on `haqa'iq`. All his books are on `hujjat` (proof) or `ta'wil` (interpretation), although his own station is very lofty.
(Mim and `Ayn` are often mentioned to refer to the `muhtajib`.)
Yes.
(Here Mim and `Ayn` are being used to refer to the `du'at`.)
Yes. Yes, yes.

*Al-'ati bi-ma la yakhtur ‘alal afkar*. (Bringing forth that which had never occurred to the thoughts.) Such things you brought forth that had never crossed the thoughts. They were not manifest in the thoughts. The books you have, *Anwar al-Shashaniyyah* and other books, the lofty realities within them, are very special things that you have presented.
*Wa saribuhu alladhi ghada ghidha' al-nufus bi-asrar al-malakut al-quddus*. (And his drinking companion, who became the nourishment of souls with the secrets of the holy `malakut`.) And your drinking companion (`sarib`). Your `sarib` means your... Sayyidna Ibrahim. He is such that he became the nourishment of souls with the secrets of the holy `malakut`. From the secrets of Allah, the Holy.
*Wa Muhammaduhu al-muhajir min baldatihi li-‘uluwwi shanih wa rutbatihi*. (And his Muhammad, the emigrant from his town for the loftiness of his station and his rank.) And Muhammad, that is, Muhammad bin Isma'il. He is the first `Da'i` who emigrated from his town to... to Najran. Until then, where was the `da'wah`? It was in Yemen. In Sana'a, in Haraz, and in Sa'fan, it was in these places, the entire `da'wah`. But he is the first `Da'i` who left Yemen and came here to Najran. For the loftiness of your station. Your station and rank is lofty. And you are the first `Da'i`, and the only `Da'i`, who was given permission to lead prayers in `ghayr jazirah` (outside the Arabian peninsula). So you led prayers in Habasha (Abyssinia). Yes. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il.
(Did the `du'at` go back again?)
Yes.
(After him, they went back to Yemen?)
Yes.
(Your passing was in Najran?)
Your passing was in Najran. And then... your... slowly, the entire `da'wah` moved towards Najran.
(No, after him there are in the list...)
A few were there. A few were there, but then they moved.
(It was at the time of Sayyidna Ghulam Hussain Sahib that they all came here to Najran.)
No, before that. Before that... before that... Sayyidna Hasan Shihab... Sayyidna Ali bin 'Ibad... he is from here... they were here.

And what did you do? You emigrated towards Najran. And you also went to Abuna. Sayyidna Muhammad bin Isma'il. So, Sayyidna Sulayman Sahib, then, in his time, he toured Abuna. And he stayed in Abuna for quite some time. Whereas you, when you came from Yemen to India, it was a journey of great hardship. It took you one year. And at that time, you traveled through various cities of Syria, and so on. So you also stayed in Abuna. And you built a mosque there too. Which still exists today. And Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan enlarged that mosque, and then later in the time of Sayyidna Hussain bin Isma'il, it was further expanded, and rooms were built for those sitting in `i'tikaf` (seclusion). It's a very magnificent mosque.
(Is it near the port?)
No, Abuna is not a port. Abuna is a mountainous area. It's very far. It's a very mountainous area, about 200 kilometers from Najran. And Sayyidna Sahib went there. I also had the chance to go there once. And the grave of Sayyidna Rashid, Rashid bin ‘Isa, is also there. So we went to the grave of Sayyidna Rashid too and performed the *ziyarat*. And Sayyidna Rashid is the one who, after becoming a `Da'i`, for six years stood in his prayer niche and performed his missed prayers. It's possible that in the early period of the `da'wah`, some of your prayers were missed. You made up for six years of prayers standing in the prayer niche. And then, after the passing of Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan, Sayyidna Hussain bin Isma'il gave one of our companions, Ahmad was his name, a boy from Yemen, he was very intelligent and a very good boy, he gave him the prayers of Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan, the prayers of four years that were missed, because you... in your last days, you became like an angel, and you couldn't keep track of your prayers. So you made up for four years of prayers. And when Sayyidna Hussain bin Hasan passed away, you came from your house barefoot to his side, and it was a considerable distance. The distance is from here to, let's say, Grand Road station. You came there barefoot.

*Sallam 'alayhim ajma'in abad al-abadin*. (Peace be upon them all, forever and ever.) May peace be upon all of them, for all time.

*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*.

*Fatiha*.
*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim...*

*Fatiha*.
*Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim...*

<!-- lecture 2 -->

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. By His name, the Exalted, the High. Wa bihi nasta'in, wa sallallahu 'ala rasulih, Sayyidna Muhammadin wa 'ala aali Muhammadin wa barik wa sallim. As it is said, it is the statement of Allah Ta'ala that, `Wa idha qara'ta al-Qur'an fasta'idh billahi min al-shaytan al-rajim` (And when you recite the Quran, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, the accursed). That when you recite the Quran, you should seek refuge from Satan the accursed, meaning, you should recite A'udhu billah. So, since this is the Book of Allah, why was the command given to seek refuge (`ta'awwudh`)? When it is said, `Wa idha qara'ta al-Qur'an` (And when you recite the Quran), that when you recite the Quran, you should seek refuge. So, what is the reason for this? It is that although this is the word of Allah, the source of guidance, but people have been led astray by this very thing. Because a `da'i` held up this `Kitabullah` (Book of Allah) and said that this is the book of Allah that has led communities astray. Meaning, those who misinterpreted it and derived wrong meanings from it, which caused the communities to go astray. So, for this reason, upon the command of Allah, while reciting the Quran and reading other `du'as` etc. that contain verses of the Quran, we also adopt the practice of `ta'awwudh` at the beginning of the `da'wah`. But...

But not while giving the adhan.

Yes, one should not do it while giving the adhan. Because there, one should only say `Bismillah`, not... do this. And in the adhan too, the two final `kalimahs` of `La ilaha illallah`, the very last `La ilaha illallah` should not be recited in the same manner as we recite the first `La ilaha illallah` with emphasis, but rather in a soft tone. There should be a difference. Because that... is related to the `ihtizaz` of a particular being (`dhat`), for which there are special prayers from us, that he is the last `mushir`, the last person who will depart from his entire circle in the blink of an eye. So, that matter will happen in the blink of an eye. Therefore, it should not be elongated.

Yes, but the... verse that is there, `wa qulil hamdu lillah...` (and say, 'All praise is for Allah...'). After that, we should recite `salawat`. Because in its verse it only says to say the `takbira`. So, one should start directly with that, shouldn't they? "Allahu Akbar." Even without `Bismillah`. Meaning, the adhan... the `kalimat` of the adhan... between them and the verse, there shouldn't be other words, logically speaking.

No, it's not like that...

Between that and the verse...

Meaning, after `wa kabbirhu takbira` (and magnify Him with great magnification)...

Yes.

Logically, one should say "Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar," right?

Yes. Either recite the `salawat` first... either recite the `salawat` first, and then `wa qulil hamdu lillah... wa kabbirhu takbira`, "Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar," like that.

That is correct in its own place, but... there is no hard and fast rule.

Then should we not say `Bismillah` either? Should we say `Bismillah` before the adhan?

It should be said... `Bismillah` should be said. There is no hard and fast rule that the adhan should start immediately after `wa kabbirhu takbira`. But before that also, we... We read this from our own side. We read it, and there is no harm in reading it.

I had asked Imam-ul-Ma'mool, and in a `tashrih` he had said that because `hamd` (praise) should come first, that is why it says `qulil hamd`. The `salawat` is recited later, so it comes later. Recite `hamd` first, do not put anything before it. `Hamd` is a higher thing, so recite `hamd` first, then recite this. He explained the order (`tartib`) like this.

Yes, yes. And another thing is that... this is the last verse of Surah Bani Isra'il. Yes. And... it is called `Ayat al-'Izz` (the Verse of Honor). Of the names that verses have, it is... Allah... it is called `Ayat al-'Izz`.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. `Allahumma wahfaz dhata al-da'i al-jami'a li-asrarihim al-mujtama'a ladayhi anwaruhum`. O Allah, protect the being of the `da'i`, who is the collector of their secrets (`asrar`) and with whom their lights (`anwar`) are gathered. This is talking about the future. Because in life, `asrar` means `riyah`. And `anwar` means `surah` (forms). In the `da'i`'s life, the `riyah` of the `hudud al-da'wah` or the `mu'minin`... and the `anwar`, meaning the `surah`, are not yet formed. And the `riyah` will emerge after death. After being buried or after passing away, the `riyah` begin to emerge, for three days. So that is a matter for later. But later, they will be gathered with you: their `asrar`, meaning their `riyah`, and gathered with him will be their `anwar`, meaning their `surah`. Yes.

`Wahmi hawzatahu`. And protect his `hawzah`. `Hawzah` is a place... around which... the earth... around which a wall has been drawn. Meaning, what is the purpose here? The center of the `da'wah`. Protect the center of the `da'wah`... `min fawaran fir'awni al-zaman` (from the aggression of the Pharaoh of the age). Because `li-kulli da'in fir'awn` (for every `da'i` there is a Pharaoh). For every `da'wah`, the person who is the `da'i` has a Pharaoh, a... an opponent of the `da'wah`. We had asked in Hujjat that during the time of Sayyidna Husayn ibn Ahmad, there didn't seem to be any such Pharaoh who was opposing the `da'i`. It was such a magnificent era... that time was a completely peaceful era. Later, all these disturbances happened. So it was said, "Well, the... `da'i` of the Dawoodis that you have is also a kind of Pharaoh." Meaning, in a passive way. He didn't openly confront, but if he had gotten the chance, he would have, like Dawood ibn Qutub, made a claim. And he would have become a claimant (`mudda'i`).

But sir, here we see that the opposition is greater.

Yes. There it wasn't, but here there is. There is a Pharaoh. Yes.

`wa ballighhu jami'a ma'moolihu ya Rahim ya Rahman` (and convey to him all his aspirations, O Rahim, O Rahman). And may Allah convey... his aspirations, his desires, the `da'i`'s... may He fulfill those desires. `Ya Rahim ya Rahman`. Now here, `ya Rahim ya Rahman` is also a `tasbih`. The `tasbih` of the Pir is... `ya Rahim ya Rahman`.

Shouldn't it be `ya Rahman ya Rahim`? `Rahim` always comes second. Yes, it is `ya Rahman ya Rahim`. Yes. But here... In the Quran, it is always `ya Rahman ya Rahim`. `Rahman` always precedes `Rahim`. But here... in books, this is not always observed because... in the way that rhyme schemes (`qafiyah-payma'i`) are done...

`Fir'awn al-zaman` came, that's why...

Yes, yes. `Fir'awn al-zaman`, `ya Rahim ya Rahman`. So, for the sake of rhyming.

In other attributes too, `Rahim` always comes second. `Ra'uf-ur-Rahim`, `Aziz-ur-Rahim`. It has never come first, except...

It has not.

But in the `sahifas`, in many places in the `du'as`, it is `Rahim` then `Rahman`.

Yes. In the `du'as`, in several places it is `Rahim` then `Rahman`.

But, but in the Quran, it is always...

In the Quran, yes. And `Rahman` has also come alone. Whereas no other name of Allah has come alone, except for `Rahman`. For example, `al-Sami' al-'Alim`, `al-Ghafur al-Rahim`. So, it has always come in a pair (`du'i`). Yes.

It has also come in the verse `Qulid'ullaha awid'ur-Rahman` (Say, 'Call upon Allah or call upon al-Rahman').

Yes. Yes.

In Surah al-Hajj, at one place it says `Ya Dhal-Jalali wal-Ikram` (O Possessor of Majesty and Honor). In Surah al-Hajj, the verses... from 58 to 65 are very blessed verses. In them... there are eight verses which are called `Mafatih al-Jinan` (Keys to the Gardens). Just as there are eight heavens, their keys are these verses. And there is great virtue in reciting them. In one verse, one sentence contains fifteen of the `Asma' al-Husna` (Beautiful Names of Allah). In it, there are fourteen... twelve verses, and the fifteenth is `Ya Dhal-Jalali wal-Ikram`. `Dhal-Jalali wal-Ikram` is taken as one.

`Rahman` is also at the end.

Yes, `Rahman` is also there. Yes, at the end.

`wa anir masabih da'watihi`. "And illuminate the lamps of his `da'wah`." Meaning, the `hudud al-da'wah`. Illuminate the whole `da'wah`. `wa kaththir 'adada shi'atihi`. "And increase the number of his followers (`shi'ah`)." `wa asri ilayna mawaddahu`. "And cause to flow towards us his substance (`mawad`)." And his confirmations (`ta'yidat`). `wa 'allimna bi-lata'ifi barakatihi`. "And teach us with the subtleties of his blessings." Meaning, the subtle things of the blessings... direct them towards us. `warzuqna tahannunahu wa shafaqatahu`. "And grant us the sustenance of this `da'i`'s kindness (`tahannun`) and compassion (`shafaqah`). `wa 'afwahu wa rahmatuhu`. "And his forgiveness (`'afw`) and pardon (`ghufran`)." That he may forgive our sins... and his mercy (`rahmah`). `Ya 'Aliyyu ya 'Azim, ya 'Aliyyu ya 'Azim`. Now, `ya 'Aliyyu ya 'Azim` is also a `tasbih`, which is done on Wednesdays.

`Wa ba'du, fa-inna-ha waradat masa'il 'aliyatun`. "And after this, there came lofty issues." And after this... that some lofty issues... were presented (`warid`), i.e., they came. They were presented... `ila al-hadrat al-samiyah`. To the elevated presence (`hadrat al-samiyah`), i.e., to the venerable presence (`buzurg hadrat`), i.e., to the `da'i`. `min ba'd al-awlad`. From some of the 'children' (`awlad`).

To your father?

Yes. That is, `hadrat al-samiyah` is Sayyidna Hibtullah al-Mu'ayyad... yes, from Sayyidna Hibtullah al-Mu'ayyad, some issues came from one of the 'children'. The questions that people, the `abna' al-da'wah` (children of the `da'wah`), ask. `Fa-sata al-amru al-'alami`. The term `al-'alami` is used for Sayyidna Hibtullah. One of his titles is `Alamuddin` (the banner of religion). The high banner of the religion. `Fa-sata al-amru`. "So the command flowed... of `al-'alami`," i.e., of the `da'i`, `ila 'abdihi akhar` (to his other servant). Upon me. Hamza b. al-Walid says... Sayyidna Ali says, it flowed upon me... `al-haqir`, `bi-an yujibu 'ala su'alatihi`. That I should answer his questions. `wa ma lahu bi-aqlamihi tatr`. "And... with his pens, to write it down as well." To give the answer, not just a verbal answer, but to put it into writing. `Wa kuntu aradtu an a'tadhira ila mawlayya li-qusuri 'ilmi wa bu'di fitrati wa fahmi`. "And I had wanted to make an excuse to my master due to the deficiency of my knowledge and the distance of my nature and understanding." And I wanted to... make an excuse to my master that my knowledge is deficient, that I am very lacking in knowledge. And in my intellect and understanding, there is great weakness. The `da'i` is saying this, Sayyidna Ali is saying this. `fa-'aradtu mawani'a ba'd al-bilad`. "And I presented the obstacles of distant lands." And I also presented... that the people from various lands who have asked questions... how can I answer them, etc. I made this excuse. `fa-asra'tu al-ta'ata`. "But in the end, I hastened to obey." I obeyed. Meaning, I accepted your command. `wa asdaqtu al-murad`. "And I found the desired object." I attained the goal. `wa tadharra'tu ila suratihi al-nuraniyah`. "And I supplicated through his luminous form (`surah nuraniyah`)." `wa awwaltu ila mawaddihi al-qudsaniyah`. "And I relied upon his holy substance (`mawad qudsaniyah`)." Yes. Now look, what a special thing has come here. That he said, "I supplicated through your luminous form." The `da'i`'s form (`surah`) has not yet been made. The `da'i` is still `natiqah`, `hissiyah`, and `namiyah`. The `surah` is only formed after passing away. But the matter of the `da'i` is such that his personality is so magnificent that we have to imagine that his very `natiqah` is the blessed `surah`. Which will become a `surah` on the Day of Resurrection.

`wa wassamtu hadhihi al-risalah bi-kunuz al-hikmah`. "And I named this treatise `Kunooz al-Hikmah` (The Treasures of Wisdom)." Treasures... which are stored for the People of `Ismah` (`arbab al-'ismah`). By `arbab al-'ismah`, it doesn't mean the Imams, but those who are the masters of partial `ismah` (`juzwi 'ismah`), who are the `du'at`. For them, these things have been stored, so that they may extract substance (`mawad`) from it. Just as it came in the introduction of this `risalah` that this matter had not been said by any `da'i` before. What matter? That the `aql al-awwal` (first intellect) and the `akhir al-mawjud` (last existent being) are equal. In what? The `aql al-awwal` and the `akhir al-mawjud`, meaning the `mustajib` who has just entered the `hadrat sharifah` and entered the circle of faith. That person. They are equal. In what? In the `ma'rifah` (gnosis) of the `Mubda'` (Originator). In the `ma'rifah` of the `Mubda' Ta'ala`, they are equal. Meaning, the `aql al-awwal`, despite being so close, has no addition in the `ma'rifah` of the `Mubda' Ta'ala`. He is perplexed and bewildered in the same way that we are perplexed and bewildered. This... no `da'i` had said this before.

`fa-ma kana fiha min sawab`. "And whatever is correct (`sawab`) in it." Meaning, whatever correctness is in this book. `fa-mansubun ilayya ila man ajra lisani`. "Then it is attributed to the one who caused my tongue to flow." Meaning, the one who gave such strength to my tongue that I could express and write it. It is attributed to him, to the `da'i`. And this `sawab`, which is `sad-waw-alif-ba`, means correctness. As I used to see in Hyderabad, when announcements were made, they would say, "You have to... yes, I will participate in this gathering." So what would they say to you? They would say `sawab`. People would write `sawab`. `Sawab` means `thawab` (reward). "Yes, it is correct. We will come." And as some people write, `Jazakallah bil-khairi was-sawab`. It's a common saying. So they write it that way. `Thawab` means the reward from Allah, the recompense. They write it with that meaning. But here `sawab` does not mean that. It means correctness. "What you said is correct."

It is a very subtle point. Yes. Everyone understood `sawab` to mean `thawab`.

`Sawab-did` is also what they say.

Yes, `sawab-did`.

I used to think `sad` meant `sahih`, like ticking something as correct.

It's not `sahih`, it's `sawab`.

`ajra sani... ajra lisani`?

`lisani`.

`ajra...`

`ila man ajra lisani`. The one who caused my tongue to flow. `wa ma kana fiha min khata', wa billahi al-'iyadh`. "And whatever mistake is in it, and in Allah I seek refuge."

Is it not `the`?

`ajra lisani`.

`lisani`. `lam-sin-alif` it is.

Your copy has `sin`. My copy has `the`.

It shouldn't be `the`.

`lisani`.

`wa billahi al-'iyadh`. "And in Allah I seek refuge. `fa-mansubun ilayya`. Then it is attributed to me." Meaning, the mistake is attributed to me, the error I made in it. `idh ana alaqtu al-jani`. "As I am a sinner (`jani`)." People sometimes call their beloved (`mahbub`) `jani`. They call their children `jani`. "My sinner." `Jani` in Arabic means "sinner."

`jaan` is what they call them, sir. `jaan`.

`jaan` also shouldn't be said.

One shouldn't even say `jaan bhi` (my life too).

`wa ana akhidhu hadh al-'ahd al-mu'akkad`. "And I am taking this confirmed covenant (`'ahd`)." `wa mithaquhu al-mughalladh al-mushaddad`. "And its pact, which is severe and strong." `'ala man waqa'a hadha fi yadayhi`. "Upon whosoever this falls into his hands." Whosoever's hands this book reaches, and who becomes aware of its secret... `an la yaqra'a fihi`. "That he should not read in it." What is written in it. `wa la yata'ammala ma'anihi`. "And he should not ponder its meanings." `illa bi-fushin min huwa al-naqid`. "Except with the permission of one who is the `naqid` (critic)." `Naqid` is one who can decide the good or bad of something. One who criticizes, who understands what is right and what is wrong. That is a `naqid`. `wal-basir`. "And the insightful one (`basir`)," who has insight. `man lahu al-taqdim wal-ta'khir`. "The one who has the authority of `taqdim` and `ta'khir` (bringing forward and putting back)." Who can bring forward someone from the `hudud` or put them back. In appointing... he has the full right. With his permission, then read it. `wa illa, fa-huwa bari'un min al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud`. "Otherwise, he is absolved from the Exalted, the Worshipped (`al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud`)." Meaning, `al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud` will know his matter.

Sir, what is meant by `al-'Aliyy al-ma'bud`?

`Al-'Aliyy` is one of the names of Allah. `Al-'Aliyy`. Allah, the Worshipped, whose worship we perform. He will know.

`wa min kaffat al-mahdudin wal-hudud`. "And from all the `mahdudin` and `hudud`." From their obedience, he is absolved. Yes.

`wa hadha qawl al-sa'il hujjatuhu`. "And this is the statement of the questioner, his argument." Who said it. The one who is writing this... in the introduction. He has written the entire question in the introduction. `awradtuhu bi-fassih ru'yatihi`. "I have presented it with the clarity of his vision." The one who... the way he asked the question to the `da'i`. `hafidhallahu al-maqam al-'azim`. "May Allah protect the great station (`maqam al-'azim`)." `wal-'alam al-rabbani al-'alim`. "And the lordly, knowing banner." The one who is a lordly scholar and who is very knowing. `Alamuddin Hibtullah b. Ibrahim al-Muqrami`. `hafidhahullahu wa dafa'a 'anhu al-aswa'`. "May Allah protect him and ward off evils from him." Now, `aswa'` (evils) - does it mean the `da'i` has evils that we are praying to be warded off? Or like... `khataya'i idhama intalat al-jibali` (my sins, when they rise like mountains). As Sayyidna Sijilmasi says, "My sins are like mountains."

`kal-rimali` (like sands).

No, no, `idhama intalat al-jibali`. "When they rise like mountains." They are so great. So there, are his sins so great? We don't consider our own sins, even big ones, to be that great. So what is the meaning of those sins? That which is said, "My sins are like mountains." So the `da'i`'s sins are actually... when they intend to do some good deed. They intend to do a good deed, but due to some obstacles, they are unable to do that good deed. It happens. It happens to us too. With us believers, it happens. We intend to do good, for example, to help this person. But in the meantime, some other thought comes, and the person leaves. So, various things like this happen. So, the good deeds they intended... to write a book, or to pen down a noble thought or concept, or to benefit someone... but for some reason, it was left undone. They consider those to be their sins. That I had the thought, but I couldn't put it into action. They consider those to be big sins. Yes.

Allah also says to the Prophet that your former and latter sins are forgiven. God forbid, the Prophet's sins?

Yes, yes. For that... what did Sayyidna Husayn b. Ismail, Qaddas Allahu Ruhahu, say? We two brothers were studying. Sayyidna Ali b. Husayn's brother, Muhsin, came. He was very thin and frail, and... he was not a great scholar himself, but he was always in the presence of the `da'i`. His brother was a `da'i`. And... he used to ask a lot of questions. Anyway, he was Muhsin's son-in-law and also his father-in-law. Muhsin b. Ali. So, he came, and Sayyidna and I were sitting and studying when Muhsin came. He said, "Malik, I have a question. Can I ask?" I said, "What is the question?" So... he said that in the Quran, in the first verse of Surah al-Fath... the first three verses: `Inna fatahna laka fathan mubina` (Indeed, We have granted you a clear victory), `li-yaghfira laka Allahu ma taqaddama min dhanbika wa ma ta'akhkhara`. "So that Allah may forgive you your former and latter sins." So, what were the Prophet's sins, the former and latter? Sayyidna looked at me and said, "Do you know?" I said, "From your substance (`mawad`), we know something about this." That the Prophet's... sins... it is not referring to the Prophet's own being. The Prophet's being is pure (`bari`). But the `hujab` (veils/deputies)... they rise from the `hudud al-da'wah` and later become `hujab`. They don't have the substance of `ismah` initially. `Ismah` comes to them later. So they are unable to control themselves completely. And sometimes they make mistakes. So, it is an allusion to them that... the former and latter sins... by `zanb` (sin) it is meant that the `hujab` of the Prophet... who was inclined towards Abu Bakr. He was inclined towards Abu Bakr. So that is a sin of the Prophet. And the second sin was that...

Giving some part of Ali's `wilayah` to Abu Bakr.

Yes, that's it. Being inclined and... He was inclined towards Abu Bakr, to the extent that he wanted... that `hujab` wanted that a portion of the `wilayah` of Ali be given to him, so that the system of the `da'wah` would continue in its place. Allah was displeased with this, and a verse came in the Quran: `Wa in ashrakta layahbatanna 'amaluka`. "And if you associate partners [with Allah], your work will surely become worthless." God forbid, to whom is this being said? The one from whom we learned the lesson of faith. It is being said to that Prophet. `Wa in ashrakta layahbatanna 'amaluka`. "Then We will nullify your work." What a tremendous thing this is! That... this is not referring to apparent shirk (polytheism). It means if you included anyone, even an iota, in the `da'wah` of Ali, in the `wilayah`, then We will nullify your work. That is the meaning of it. So here it is said that all your matters and [unclear], We have completely forgiven them.

What is the translation of `tasham?`

Yes, yes. It's very...

It's about past sins. What about future sins?

That... I don't recall it at the moment. It's about past sins. And future sins are also mentioned.

...so `wa hadha qawl al-sa'il hujjatuhu awradtuhu bi-fassihi wa hay'atihi`. `wa dafa'a 'anhu al-aswa'`. `Aswa'` means that which... the evils are actually the `mahdudin` and the `hudud al-da'wah`. Yes. They are actually the evils.

`wa ballighhu kulla ma yarumu wa yahwa`. "And convey to him everything that he intends and desires." And may Allah convey... whatever you intend and whatever you desire, may He fulfill that desire. `wa 'alayhi min 'abdihi afdhal al-salam`. "And upon him from his servant, the best of peace." `wal-matlub min tafaddulihi jawab ma sa'altu 'anhu`. "And what is requested from his grace is the answer to what I have asked about." And it is requested that... from your grace, you answer what I have asked. These are the words of the questioner. `bi-hadhb al-manni 'alayya wal-tafadduli wal-tatawwuli`. "By the favor of grace upon me, and grace and bounty." The grace that you want to bestow upon me. `wa uqaddimu bayna yadayhi ma as'alu 'anhu ilayhi`. "And I present before him what I ask about to him." of the `wilayah` of the `hudud` of your religion. `wa al-dallina 'alayhi`. "And those who guide towards him." `wa abra'u mimman ankara addanihum`. "And I absolve myself from those who deny their lowest ones." Meaning, the `hudud al-din`. `wa atadharra'u ilayhi bi-wusuli 'alam al-ibda' al-kiram`. "And I supplicate to him through the connection of the noble world of creation." Who are the honorable ones. `wa bi-haqqi kulli wasiyyin 'azimin wa imamin`. "And by the right of every great successor (`wasi`) and Imam." `wa bi-kulli da'iratin tadawwarat`. "And by every circle that revolved." The various circles that... of Imamate, of `qa'imiyat`... the circles. `wa nuktatin sharifatin takawwanat`. "And a noble point that came into being." `an la...`

What is the word?

`tahjibuni`.

`tahjibuni`. That you may not... put me aside, seeing the weakness of my master. `fa-mathaluka man yasturu 'ala misli`. "For your example is one who covers [the faults] of someone like me." Of someone like me. `wa idha tafaddalta an la tukhayyibni mimma ammaltu`. "And when you show grace, that you may not disappoint me in what I have hoped for." `bi-haqqika al-'azim la tahmalu haqqi`. "By your great right, do not neglect my right." Meaning, do not waste me. By not giving an answer. `an... wa an tarhamani birrika wa 'ammika`. And your piety (`bir`) and your beneficence (`'am`)... `sirrika`... and your `safaqa`, your forgiveness (`'afw`) and your `awn`, help, and your `ghawth`, your kindness, in the two worlds (`darayn`), meaning this world and the hereafter. `wal-nazar ila ma afuh bihi nafsan aw tafawwuhan`. And what I will say and what my soul is filled with, or uttered... `la yujidu lahu ma'na`. "Which has no meaning." Meaning, I am asking questions, and in them such things will also come in which my soul is involved, or uttered. So for me... `fa-albisni sawb al-sitr wal-ghina'`. "Then clothe me with the garment of concealment (`sitr`) and contentment (`ghina'`)." Put the veils on me. `wa umnun 'alayya bi-tatabu'i ihsanika`. "And favor me with the continuity of your benevolence." `razaqani Allahu ridaka`. "May Allah grant me the sustenance of your pleasure (`rida`)." `wa qad lakam a'udhu billahi wa bil-'ali`. "And indeed, I seek refuge in Allah and in the Exalted... and from the lofty ones." From them. `An la yadhunna al-dhannu anni ma as'alu illa mutaddi'an fasaahat lisan wa ghadharat jinan`. "That the one who thinks may not think that I am asking only as a claimant of eloquence of tongue or sharpness of mind." "No, by Allah, never." `wa innama qasartu 'an al-shurbi min ra's al-ma'in`. "But rather, I fell short of drinking from the head of the spring." Meaning... `ra's al-ma'in` means... from the `da'i` himself, from benefiting directly from him, I was deprived. `wa qad 'alima al-hatm al-'azim al-barr al-rahim bi-aqdari 'abdihi wa [unclear]`. And the great, pious, merciful decree knew... that which the `qa'id` has stated... that `kaifa wa sadqa min sadaqatihi`. That whatever you bestow upon me, it will be a charity (`sadaqah`) from your charities. `wa nafkhatan min nafahatihi`. "And a breath from his breaths." It will be a forgiveness from your forgiveness. `wa la akhira qurbanan illa ma qaddamtu min masturihi`. "And I do not seek any other nearness except what I have presented from his written work." I have presented it to you. That's all.

So, he is saying here that... what existed before the `Ibda'`, he is asking, and along with that, he is also talking about after... so, the questioner has asked, one question he asked is what was there before the `'Alam al-Ibda'`. And the `uqul `Alam al-Ibda'` that came, what were their circumstances, what happened to them? And he is saying that the `hijab al-khass` (special veil) is made from the `riyad al-hadrah` (gardens of the presence). He has presented his own concept. Now, there are three types of `hijabs`. So how has it been placed in the `tasbih`? That we first do in the `tasbih`... `Allahu Akbar` (God is the greatest). Right? When we say `Allahu Akbar`, that points towards the `hijab al-adna` (lowest veil), which is called the `hijab al-tabi'i` (natural veil) or `hijab al-tanasuli` (procreative veil). And then the second part of 33, that is the `hijab al-dhilli` (shadowy veil). And the third part, of `Subhanallah` (Glory be to God), that is the `hijab al-khass` (special veil). The `hijab al-khass`. And in the end, we come to the Imam, who is the `dhat-i Imam` (essence of the Imam). So, we pass through these three states. So, the one who is speaking says that the `hijab al-khass` of the Imam is made from the `riyad` (gardens) of the `hadarat` (presences). He has presented his own concept. `wa hijab al-la'iq al-tabi'i` (And the corresponding natural veil)... and the veil that comes after it, that is connected to it, is the `tabi'i` (natural) one, `min riyad hujaj al-jaza'ir` (from the gardens of the Hujaj of the Islands), which is made from the `riyad` of the `hujaj al-jaza'ir` (Proofs of the Islands). `ta'wi ila ba'd nisa' al-hijab al-zawahir` (resorting to some of the women of the outward veil)... and which... this `hijab al-tabi'i` is... they come... from some women, `al-hijab al-zawahir 'ala hadhihi al-kayfiyyah` (the outward veil upon this modality)... with their outward veils. Meaning, that those who perform the act of marriage with their wives, who does that? The `hijab al-tabi'i` (natural veil) of the Imam does that. That is his point. That the `tabi'i` veil does it. `ta'wi ila ba'd nisa' al-hijab al-zawahir 'ala hadhihi al-kayfiyyah wujuduhu` (resorting to some of the women of the outward veil upon this modality of its existence). Its existence is in this modality. `wa yadharu bi-khalqihi wa min khalqihi fi khalqihi` (And he appears through his creation, from his creation, and in his creation). And that veil appears from creation, from the side of creation, and in creation. Meaning, that it is made from `riyad` (gardens), so who are the `riyad`? They are from the creatures (`makhluqat`). And it... then... the `hijabs`... after the veils are made, they are formed, and then they come into human form, those veils, the `riyad`. The `riyad` come in human form. It is pointing towards that. `wa kayfa kana qabla wujud al-samawat wa al-ard` (And how was it before the existence of the heavens and the earth)... and before the existence of the skies... the heavens and the earth, what things were there? `wa qad 'alimtu mimma wujudha` (and I have known from what its existence is)... and I know that their... the heavens' and the earth's existence... how did it happen? `wa min ayyi fudulatin` (and from what remnants)... and from what remnants did it happen? `wujud al-awwal al-jussa' fi safil al-ard` (The existence of the first particle in the low part of the earth). And that... from what remnants... the `awwal jussa'` (first particle)... `awwal jussa'` means the first particle of the `karr`, its existence... from what remnants did it happen? Which was created from the clod in the earth. The one that comes at the beginning of the `karr`, it is created from the clod in the earth. So, it has a great station. So... that... from what remnants did it happen? Because... there... there was nothing then. So from what remnants does it come? `fa-tafaddal bi al-jawab` (So graciously provide the answer). You should benefit me with the answer... and with the explanation. `Al-jawab 'ala dhalika al-su'al` (The answer to that question). Now the Da'i is saying... Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah is saying that... in response to your question, I am giving the answer. `naqulu bi-'awnillah al-kabir al-muta'al` (We say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted). We say, with the help of Allah, who is the `kabir al-muta'al` (Great, Exalted). `Al-kabir al-muta'al` is also a `tasbih` that we have, which is recited on Thursdays. `wa minnati waliyy al-'asr wa al-zaman` (and from the favor of the master of the age and time)... and from the favor of the `wali al-'asr wa al-zaman` (master of the age and time)... `alayhi salawat al-rahim al-rahman` (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). Upon you be the blessings of the `rahim al-rahman` (Merciful, Compassionate). Here `rahim` came first. In the Quran, `rahim` has never come first, `rahman` has always come first. `wa ta'yidi mawlay al-'alam` (and with the support of my master of the world)... because `rahman`, in `Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim`, the various interpretations... in that, `al-rahman` also refers to Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib. In one interpretation, it points to Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib, and `rahim` is for Mawlana Abu Talib. Yes. So, Mawlana 'Abd al-Muttalib was certainly greater than Mawlana Abu Talib. He is the `jadd al-qa'im` (grandfather of the Qa'im). The `jadd al-'ayn` (grandfather of the 'ayn) and the `jadd al-'ayn al-akhirah` (grandfather of the final 'ayn) as well. Yes. `wa ta'yidi mawlay al-'alam` (and with the support of my master of the world)... and with the support of `mawlay al-'alam` (my master of the world)... `wa sirrihi al-bahir al-mu'azzam` (and his manifest and magnified secret)... and your... that which is a manifest and magnified secret, from its support, I am giving the answer to this matter, that `anna al-jawab 'ala dhalika qad sabaqa min mawla al-malik 'alam al-iman` (the answer to that has already been given by the master, the king, the flag of faith). So this answer has already passed from... in the book of Sayyidna Hibatullah, in `Hibat al-Rahman`, which you have written, `fi kitabihi al-sharif al-mawsum bi-kanz al-'ulum` (in his noble book, named Kanz al-'Ulum [The Treasure of Knowledge])... in your book, `Kanz al-'Ulum`, in that... this answer to your question is there. This is a very special book, Inshallah, if we get a chance, we will bring it. In that... I remember that in `Kanz al-'Ulum`, you have written that about Bibi Fatima al-Zahra, it is written that her station and greatness are such that even the A'immah Tahirin (the pure Imams) could not attain her gnosis. Yes, and for this very reason, the later Imams are called *A'immah Fatimiyyin* (the Fatimid Imams). The later Imams, who are up to the 21st Imam, they are called *A'immah Fatimiyyin* because they are perplexed and bewildered by the station of Bibi Fatima al-Zahra. For this reason, they are called *A'immah Fatimiyyin*. `'alayka ya ayyuha al-arshad bi al-jawab al-awwala 'ala al-awwal` (Upon you, O rightly guided one, is the first answer to the first [question]). ...your first... the answer to your first question is `bi al-qawl al-'ali al-mumajjad` (with the high and glorified saying)... that the saying is such that it is high and great, `ma fihi kifayah kafiyah` (in which there is sufficient sufficiency)... and in it there is sufficiency, `wa ni'ma al-shafiyah` (and what an excellent cure)... and it is a cure, which is hidden, that `anna wujud 'alam al-ibda' la min shay'in` (the existence of the World of Origination is not from a thing)... ...`wujud 'alam al-ibda' la min shay'in wa-la fi shay'in wa-la yajibu al-bahth 'an kayfiyyatihi` (the existence of the World of Origination is not from a thing, and not in a thing, and it is not required to inquire about its modality). So, this `'Alam al-Ibda'`... meaning, the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) created the `'Alam al-Ibda'`. So, our concept is that there would be a world in which the intellects were created. Right? But it is not like that. Mawlana 'Iddan Sahib also used to say this, that the world and the intellects are not separate things. Rather, `'alam al-ibda' hum al-'uqul` (the 'Alam al-Ibda' is the Intellects themselves). The `'Alam al-Ibda'`, they themselves are the intellects, whom Allah Ta'ala created. Yes. So, the `'Alam al-Ibda'` is without any thing and without being in any thing. `wa-la yajibu...` Therefore, it is not obligatory, it is not permissible, that you should inquire about the modality of the `sudur al-ibda'` (emanation of the origination), how... how the `ibda'` came into existence. `wa-la su'al idh laysa li al-'aql fihi majal` (and no question, since the intellect has no scope in it). That... our intellects cannot go there. How the `ibda'`... emanated. How the `ibda'` was created. How the `ibda'` appeared. This is a matter far beyond our intellects. `wa fi al-ajwibah 'an dhalika dalal wa ishrak` (and in the answers to that is misguidance and polytheism). And if someone gives an answer about this, that it happens like this, it happens like that, the `ibda'`, then that is misguidance and `shirk` (polytheism). `wa tahawwutun fi bahr al-halak` (and a plunge into the sea of destruction). And he will fall into the sea of destruction, the one who speaks. Because we do not even know how the `'Alam al-Ibda'` came into existence. `na'udhu billahi min dhalika` (We seek refuge in Allah from that). We seek refuge in Allah from that thing. `wa nas'aluhu al-nijah min al-mahalik` (and we ask Him for salvation from the places of destruction). And we ask for salvation, that we may be destroyed in that matter, we ask for salvation from it. `li-anna dhalika mu'allal fi-man la tajasar naḥwahu al-khawatir` (because that is substantiated for one towards whom thoughts do not dare to venture). Because that matter is proven by evidence that... and that matter is such that `khawatir`, that is, the heart, cannot dare to go towards it, how... the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) created the `'Alam al-Ibda'`. And what was that modality? We cannot have knowledge of its modality. `wa-la tuhitu bihi al-awham wa al-dama'ir` (and estimations and inner thoughts do not encompass it). And `awham` (estimations) and `dama'ir` (inner thoughts) cannot encompass it. That modality. `wa yahtaj al-'arif li-dhalika 'ala an yakuna ma'ahu surah aw 'aql aw wahm qabla huduth al-ibda'` (And the gnostic needs for that [knowledge] that he should have with him a form, an intellect, or an estimation from before the origination of the 'ibda'). And the gnostic needs that... he should have... some form, intellect or estimation... that what was the matter before the `ibda'`. `aw fikrah bal suduruhu 'an al-huwiyyah al-muta'aliyah` (or a thought; rather its emanation is from the Exalted Identity). One should just know this much, that the emanation of the `'Alam al-Ibda'`... the `huwiyyah al-muta'aliyah` (Exalted Identity), meaning, the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted), happened through His... means. `la min shay'in` (not from a thing). And it did not happen from any thing. `wa qad awdaha al-du'at sababu qillati al-wusul ila ma'rifati dhalika` (And the du'at have clarified the reason for the scarcity of access to the gnosis of that). And the `du'at` (preachers) have explained, have clarified that our access to its gnosis is almost non-existent. `dhalika li-'ajz al-'uqul al-ibda'iyyah` (that is due to the inability of the originated intellects). And these `uqul ibda'iyyah` (originated intellects), whom the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` (Originator, the Exalted) has created, meaning the First Intellect, the Second Intellect, etc., the `uqul ibda'iyyah`... they themselves are at a loss to know that how... this... this, the `Mubdi' Ta'ala` has done. They themselves are at a loss. `fa-kayfa bi-man dunahum min al-suwar al-daniyah` (So how about those below them from the lowly forms?). So how can it be that the lowly forms... our forms are very lowly compared to them. Compared to the intellects of the `'Alam al-Ibda'`, our forms are very lowly. So how can it be that we can know that modality? `wa amma al-hijab al-'ali alladhi huwa al-nasut al-khass al-hawi li al-sharaf wa al-ikhtisas` (As for the high veil, which is the special human form, containing honor and specialty)... and the `hijab al-'ali` (high veil) is, which is the `nasut al-khass` (special human form)... such that it contains honor and specialty, meaning it is special, and it is a `khass` (special) one. `wa al-nasut al-tabi'i al-dani` (and the natural, lowly human form)... and the `nasut al-tabi'i` (natural human form) which is lowly... which is the lowest `nasut`... `fa-khudh haqiqata ma'rifatiha min awwal al-tamhid` (So take the reality of its gnosis from the beginning of the preface). So take the reality of it from the first preface. Meaning, which preface? In this book, at the very beginning, he has started that, "All praise is for Allah, who made the `nasut al-khass` from the `riyadat ahl al-ikhtisas` (gardens of the people of specialty)." That the `nasut al-khass` is made from... the `ahl al-ikhtisas` (people of specialty). Meaning, the `hadarat` (presences) and the `hudud` (ranks) of the da'wah, from their `riyadat` (spiritual gardens), it is made. This... `ahl al-ikhtisas` is said for them. `wa ja'ala al-nasut al-dani min riyadat man dunahum min al-mawani` (And He made the lowly human form from the gardens of those below them in station). And the `nasut`... which is lowly, meaning `tabi'i` (natural), that is made from those who are lesser than them. So, this... the one who asked the question... that the `hujaj al-jaza'ir`... the `nasut al-tabi'i` is made from the `hujaj al-jaza'ir`. So you have said that this is a wrong concept of yours. You will understand its reality from the first preface. `tataddahu laka ma'ani` (its meanings will become clear to you). The meanings will become clear to you... its meanings and secrets. `wa sun dhalika yasunka Allah fa-huwa min al-sirr al-rabbani` (And guard that, may Allah guard you, for it is from the Lordly secret). And you protect this thing, and Allah will protect you. It is from the `sirr al-rabbani` (Lordly secret). And these things... meaning, about the veils and the `nasut`, how they originated and from what they are made, this is a great `sirr al-rabbani`. `wa amma qawluka inna al-nasut al-tabi'i al-zahir min hujaj al-jaza'ir fa-huwa minka ghalat` (As for your saying that the manifest natural human form is from the Hujaj of the Islands, that is a mistake on your part). And you... your concept, your statement, that the `nasut al-tabi'i` (natural human form) which is very manifest... is made from the `hujaj al-jaza'ir` (Proofs of the Islands), so this concept of yours is wrong. He is saying. `wa man i'taqada dhalika fa-qad taradda wa saqat` (And whoever believes that has been rejected and has fallen). And whoever believes in this way, as you have done, is rejected and will fall. `bal al-awwal fi dhalika ayyuha al-sa'il innahu yajtami'u fi kulli nasutin riyadatu jami' al-sa'irin ila al-lahut` (Rather, the first [principle] in that, O questioner, is that in every human form gather the spiritual gardens of all the travelers to the Divine Realm). And... that in every `nasut`, in every `nasut`, the `riyadat` (spiritual gardens)... of those who are traveling... `ila al-lahut` (to the Divine Realm)... meaning, of all believers... `min 'alin wa danin` (from the high and the low). Whether he is high or low. Yes. `hasbama amma 'anhu sayyiduna al-da'i sarimuddin` (according to what our master, the Da'i Sarimuddin, implied)... as Sayyidna Sarimuddin, meaning Sayyidna Ibrahim ibn Muhammad ibn Fahd, has made it understood, has explained, that `ba'd kutubihi al-'aliyah` (in some of his high books)... in your some of his high books... `wa ghayruhu min mawalinna al-du'at 'ala al-lawh al-sharif al-altaf` (and others from our masters, the du'at, upon the noble and subtle tablet)... and besides them also, the `du'at`, they have also pointed towards this matter, `fi al-majam' al-nuraniyyah` (in the luminous collections), that the `surat`... the `nasut` that ascend... inside the `nasut`, the `riyadat` ascend, and inside the `lahut`, the `surat` (forms) ascend. This has been mentioned. Meaning, the point of saying this is that... the `nasut` of the Imam, the `nasut` of the Imam, in which the `riyadat` are ascending... and the `lahut` of the Imam, in which the `surat` are ascending, they are parallel. It is not that in the Imam's `nasut`, the `riyadat` that come at the place of the ear... then in the `lahut`, those `surat` come to a different place, like the place of the heart, or a lower place. It is not like that. At whichever place the `riyadat` go in the `nasut`, at that same place, the `surat` will go in the `lahut`. This is what he means to say. `murad al-hijab al-khass huwa al-mubtana fi al-bayt al-haram` (The intended meaning of the special veil is that which is built in the Sacred House). And the `nasut-i khass` (special human form), its foundation is laid in the `Bayt al-Haram` (Sacred House of God). That is, `huwa alladhi la ta'zibuhu qara'ib al-aslab wa al-arham` (it is that which the proximities of loins and wombs do not confine). And this `nasut-i khass` is not bound by the loins and wombs. Meaning, it is not born from a mother and father. Its construction, its making, happens in the Sacred House of God. Yes. But the `tabi'i` (natural) one is born from a mother and father. `wa-la tahwih afniyah wa-la dur` (and neither courtyards nor houses contain it). It... any... courtyard... meaning, any ground... or any... house... it cannot contain it. The `nasut-i khass` is so high that it is beyond all these. That it cannot be contained. `wa-la yaliju fi 'iḍam al-qubur` (And it does not enter into the bones of the graves). And nor does it descend into the graves. Meaning, at the time of death, in death, the `nasut-i khass` does not even descend into the graves. It is the Imam's affair, right? Yes, and it does not... `wa-lladhi yaqa'u 'alayhi al-mawt wa al-qatl wa al-fawt` (And the one upon whom death, killing, and passing away occur)... and the one upon whom death occurs, and killing occurs, `wa al-fawt` (and passing away) occurs, the person passes away... `huwa adna min al-hujub` (it is the lowest of the veils). That is the lowest among the veils. `li-yaqdiya ma 'alayhi wa yajib` (so that it may fulfill what is upon it and is obligatory). So, the states that befall it, it is on this... this lowest veil that they befall, not on the `nasut-i khass`. Just as it happened with Imam Husayn. The killing that happened, whatever happened, nothing happened to the real self. Whatever happened, happened to your veils. The real self is protected. The hijab, which one was it at that time? There is no mention of that. No, there is no mention of it. Hadrat Gyani Sahib muqaddas also used to ask if you have read anywhere which veil came into use and when. So, it has not been read anywhere, which veil came and when. However, for Rasulullah salawatullah 'alayh, it is that your `ghusl` (ritual bath) veil was different, and the one that was buried was different. That the `ghusl` veil was 'Abdullah ibn Mas'ud. Yes. And the one who was buried was 'Abdullah ibn Rawahah. Yes. And the one present for `ziyarat` (visitation) is Ubayy ibn Ka'b. Yes. And these are their own `hijabs` that are buried. Or... so this is... and Ubayy ibn Ka'b has a great station. Ubayy ibn Ka'b is also a teacher of Rasulullah. As it is said about you... you... Rasulullah himself said that Ubayy is my spiritual mother (`ummi al-ruhaniyyah`). That... the first teacher you had was Ubayy ibn Ka'b, who did what? He taught you all the laws and principles of Adam alayhis salam. He taught them. So, you became `mamnun` (indebted). After that... after that, you became `mamnun`. Similarly, five teachers taught you, and after the teaching, they became `mamnun`. After the `ba'thah`... and the final teacher was Janab-i Khadijah. Al-Walid... Janab-i... yes, Janab-i Khadijah. Before the `ba'thah`... This is before the `ba'thah`. Yes. And... and for this reason, what happened? Janab-i Khadijah's case is that she became `mamnun` to you, and you... you not only became the teacher of Sayyidna Rasulullah, but also the teacher of Janab-i 'Ali. Yes. So, for this reason it is said... yes, she became the teacher of Janab-i 'Ali. For this reason it is said that `Ibn Maryam` (son of Mary). `Ibn Maryam` is who? So, `Isa Ibn Maryam` (Jesus son of Mary) is Janab-i 'Ali. And `Maryam` is Janab-i Khadijah. Yes. And for this very reason... for this very reason, Janab-i Maryam was exempted from the five prayers. The reason was this. The five prayers were exempted. The Prophet... after the five prayers, Janab-i 'Ali joined. Yes. Because of her being his teacher.

But I... Ubayy ibn Ka'b and all these who were `hijabs`, did they also have an idea that they were `hijabs` in the form of... yes, absolutely. Without `ma'rifah` (gnosis) it cannot be. `Ma'rifah` is a very essential thing. Yes, they knew. Yes. And great... they are people of great station. If they were to manifest, they themselves would manifest as prophets and messengers. They are people of such high station. But we have never seen in any incidents that the Prophet is teaching them or anything. From heart to heart. It was given from heart to heart. Yes. To the people... Yes. They taught. I mean, in the entire 13 years of life in Mecca, the dominance of the disbelievers and polytheists was so great. And in the 10 years in Medina, most of the time was spent in battles. Yes. But the ethical teachings that the Prophet gave, in that, as you... you used to give a lesson, the Prophet alayhis salam... in Medina... after the prayers... he would sit... after the Fajr prayer, and would ask, "Did any of you see a dream at night?" He would ask this. So someone would say that yes, I saw a dream. So you would say that I am a better interpreter than Yusuf alayhis salam. I am an interpreter. So, then you would interpret. Yes. And then when you would give a lesson, in that lesson, the disbelievers and polytheists... no, the hypocrites would also be sitting, and Muslims also, and great personalities like Salman al-Farsi would also be sitting. So what the Prophet would say, every person would gain knowledge from it according to his own level. The Prophet would say something, and its subtleties and realities would be received by whomever they were meant for. So this was the beauty of the Prophet's discourse, that whoever sought, would receive it.

And this Sayyidna Ubayy ibn Ka'b is the one about whom there are incidents that Rasulullah... once... asked Ubayy ibn Ka'b, "O Ubayy, do you know which is the best verse of the Quran? You are the best reciter (`qari`). You used to recite very well. So you are a great `qari` and you recite the Quran a lot. So which is the most excellent verse?" So he said, "Allah and His Messenger know best." So he said, "It is Ayat al-Kursi." Rasulullah said that at that time. He said, Ayat al-Kursi. And then, once what happened was that Rasulullah... was praying... and Ubayy ibn Ka'b was also praying. So... probably Rasulullah was not praying, Ubayy ibn Ka'b must have been praying some supererogatory prayer. And Rasulullah was sitting in the mihrab. So you called out to Ubayy, "Ya Ubayy!" So Janab-i Ubayy heard the voice. So then... he continued his prayer. Then the second or third time also he called. And then he quickly finished his salutations and came.
