Now the answer is this: *inna intiqaala azdad al-a'immati wa al-nutaqa', wa al-tabi'ina bihim min al-'awam, awan al-ayyam, ila tilka al-atraf al-mash'umah wa al-jihat al-mal'umah, tarmihim ilayha al-'inayat al-ilahiyyah* (Verily, the transmigration of the opponents of the Imams and the Natiqs, and their followers from among the common folk, at the time of the days, to those inauspicious peripheries and the cursed directions, the divine providence throws them there). That the opponents of the A'immah and the Nutaqa' who are very great, their transmigration and the followers of them from among the lowly people, their destination is in those peripheral places which are very bad places, and those directions which are very cursed directions. So the divine providence (*'inayat ilahiyyah*), that is, the divine arrangements, that is, he who has become the viceroy of the Tenth in the earth, that is, the *hudud siddiqiyyah*, throws them to these places. *Wa al-qudrat al-rabbaniyyah* (and the lordly power). And the lordly power carries out this action. *Fa-yakununa fiha 'ala mithali hayawan wa'l-jibal* (So they are in it in the likeness of animals and mountains). And there, they are in the likeness of animals and mountains. As it is said in another book, that there are such large chunks of ice, and it turns out that that chunk is a chunk of ice, and under it is an animal, which is such a large animal that the ice has covered it. Or there are large mountains inside which their filthy refuse gets collected.

*Wa al-hissu mawjudun fihim* (And sense perception is present in them). And in whomever these things are happening, sense perception is present in them. Yes, they feel it. Just as it was said that the person who does not take the *'ahd sharif* (noble covenant), his *nafs al-hissiyyah* (sensory soul) pervades his body, because of which he remains in a state of torment after death and feels the torments. And the same *nafs al-hissiyyah*, when we say *Allahu Akbar* and perform *dhabihah* (slaughter), then in that *dhabihah*, what is happening? We are separating the *nafs al-hissiyyah* of that goat we are slaughtering from it. Because of which it is elevated. One day it will come into a human form. Yes. And because of the separation of the *nafs al-hissiyyah*, its meat is lawful (*halal*). But it is also this, it has also been said that one should not eat the meat raw, even if the *nafs al-hissiyyah* has been separated from it, but it should be cooked and eaten. And even after cooking, you, the Rasoolullah, says that when the steam rises after cooking, it should be allowed to escape. It should not be eaten in a hot state. Because the remaining *nafs al-hissiyyah* that is left, that remainder is escaping through the steam. Yes.

*fihim, bila intiqal, muqabbalina bi'l-salasil* (in them, without transmigration, shackled with chains). And they are wearing fetters, *wa'l-aghlal* (and shackles), and they are wearing handcuffs, *mu'adhdhabina bi'l-'adhab al-ha'il al-muhin* (tormented with the terrifying, humiliating punishment). And they are punished with a great, dreadful, and horrifying punishment, *bi'l-harri wa'l-bard* (with heat and cold). With extreme heat and cold, they are punished. And as it is said that those who reach *saqar* (hellfire), they are given the light of the moon during the day as punishment, and the light of the sun at night. Yes.

*al-mu'lim al-nakil. Wa amma qawluka wa hal hiya ajsadun am al-nufus al-marmiyyah fi al-bilad wa al-bu's* (the painful, exemplary [punishment]. As for your statement, "Are they bodies or the souls cast into the lands and misery?"). That your question was that here, in these places, do their bodies reach or their souls reach? That are thrown with calamity and hardship. *Fa'lam, fa-inna-ma yufariqu ahl hadhihi al-tabaqat illa al-tasawwur al-khabith al-qa'im min nasb li-awliya' Allah wa'l-adawat* (So know, for nothing separates the people of these classes except the vile conception arising from enmity towards the friends of Allah and the enmities). Then know that what separates from these classes? Their vile conception separates. The one who has a wrong conception, that vile conception reaches those places. *Al-qa'im min nasb li-awliya' Allah wa'l-adawat* (arising from enmity towards the friends of Allah and the enmities). That which happens, which is done in enmity with the friends of Allah. *Wa amm al-nufus fa-hiya tashrif fi tilka al-ajsad al-radi'ah* (As for the souls, they are ennobled in those base bodies). As for the souls, the souls are pervaded within their bodies, which are base. *Wa yakunu minha fiha al-wayl wa al-thubur* (And from it, in it, is woe and destruction). Because of which there is ruination and destruction and affliction for him. *Wa takunu akthafa min al-ajsad li-'adhami ma a'taw* (And they become denser than the bodies because of the greatness of what they were given). And they become denser than bodies, because what the Qudrat (Power) had given, you see, Qudrat, after a period of time, progressing, it makes his soul from his bodies. From bodies, it makes his soul. And then this person, out of his foolishness, is putting his soul back into his body. The favor that the Qudrat did, he did not accept that favor and... As it is said in this, that *al-shirku zulmun 'azim* (verily, shirk is a great injustice). That shirk is a great injustice. And Allah ta'ala will forgive every sin, from the greatest to the greatest, but except for shirk. As it is said, *inna Allaha yaghfiru al-dhunuba jami'an* (Verily, Allah forgives all sins). That Allah has the power to forgive every sin, but He will not forgive shirk. Yes.

Here, the state is *la yahya wa la yamut* (neither living nor dead). Yes. Here, when they go into punishment, it is *la yahya wa la yamut*. Just as in Surah al-A'la, it is *la yamutu fiha wa la yahya* (he will neither die therein nor live). They neither live nor die. It has been said about them that in their punishments, every day they are killed a thousand times and then revived a thousand times. Yes, this is the state. How much torment there must be, and how much pain they are in.

This writer must not have been a great scholar; he has made many mistakes. This is *wa al-wayl wa al-thubur*, but here *shol* is written. Amazing. Yes, I also see many mistakes have been made by the scribes. In the writing.

*Wa amma qawluka 'ala kam mudad yulhaquna...* (As for your statement, for how long a period are they attached...). Now here, *kam madad* is written. See? It is *mudadd*, meaning the plural of *muddat* is *mudadd*. And *madad* is a different thing. For how many ages will they be attached to those peripheries? That when will they reach there? *Fa-ba'da an yaktatifu adhraw al-silsilah fi kulli nawmin minha sab'ina qamisan bila khilaf* (So after they have snatched the arms of the chain in each sleep of it seventy shirts without disagreement). And what I have learned from it is in the books of the *awliya' Allah* and the *hudat*, upon them be the salutations of their Imam, and blessings be upon them. As for your statement about the winds, they are different in all states. And among them are some ascending noble ones. And among them, some winds also ascend. *Ittasalat bi-kulli latif* (connected with every subtle thing). Which meet with some subtleties. *Wa ma kana min al-habitin ittasala bi-ma yula'imuhu min al-zalimin* (And what was of the descending ones connected with what suits it from among the oppressors). And among them, there are also descending winds, which... their connection is with the oppressors. *Wa qad yattasilu al-salihu bi'l-talih* (And the righteous may connect with the corrupt). And until the righteous connects with the corrupt, I don't know what this means, *wa yakhussu bi'l-qawl al-wadih li-mujibat qadhat ba'da asbabin* (and he specifies with the clear statement for the requirements that some causes have decreed). For what reason it happens, Allah knows best. *Min ahl al-jahl wa al-iktisab. Fa-khudh dhalika bi'l-wajiz min al-qawl* (from the people of ignorance and acquisition. So take that with the concise statement). So take this from the concise statement I have mentioned. *Wa lillahi subhanahu al-quwwah wa al-hawl* (And to Allah, the Glorified, belongs the power and the might). And for Allah alone is the power and the might. Yes. And we who say, *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim* (there is no power and no strength except in Allah, the Most High, the Most Great), this is not a verse of the Quran Majid. The verse of the Quran Majid is what? As perhaps it is in Surah Bani Isra'il, *la quwwata illa ma sha' Allah* (there is no power except what Allah wills). This is it. *La quwwata illa ma sha' Allah*. But when the Master of the Universe started using *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billah* in his du'as...

...*la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim*, when he started using this in his du'as, then this saying became so famous and Allah ta'ala liked it so much that He made it common in du'as. You will see in du'as, often in du'as, this comes at the end, *la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahi al-'aliyyi al-'azim*. And the realities of it that have been explained are that *la hawla wa la quwwata*, one meaning is that I have no strength to refrain from evil and to do good, except what Allah ta'ala grants. This is the external meaning. The other is that *la hawla wa la quwwata*, that there is no power, no might, except that which is through Allah, *al-'Aliyy al-'Azim*. So in that, what has been said? That there is no power in the external realm (*bab-i zahir*), nor in the internal realm (*bab-i batin*), to be able to form a *haykal* (spiritual form). That is, the *suwarat al-nayyirah* (luminous forms) do not go to the Imam. The form of the deceased, one day, through the *hudud daniyyah* and *hudud 'aliyyah*, it gathers with the *da'i*, then up to the point that it reaches the *Bab al-Abwab* (Gate of Gates), which is the *bahr al-zakhar* (overflowing ocean), which is called *fasl al-khitab* (the decisive speech). So there, all the forms of the believers gather with the *Bab al-Abwab*. And it is a large gathering. So within that, despite such a large gathering, it does not have the form of a *haykal*. A *haykal* is that which is in human form. But when this gathering is prepared, then the Imam, 'alayhis salam, the present Imam, receives that gathering. And he grants it to his son. So the son, who until now... finished.

...that gathering, which has gathered at the *Bab al-Abwab*... the *Bab al-Abwab*... Yes, it's repeating.

Hello. Is the voice coming? Yes. The voice is coming. Yes.

That gathering which has gathered at the *Bab al-Abwab*, the present Imam receives that gathering and grants it to his son. So the son, who until now was Imam, and this, what happened to him? He received the *imamah*. So the *imamah* is connected to the Imam. Before, he was Imam *bi'l-quwwah* (potentially). Now this gathering, being connected to him, he becomes Imam *bi'l-fi'l* (actually). And the present Imam passes away. And his *Bab al-Abwab* also passes away. And the *qalb al-haykal* (heart of the *haykal*) is formed of the son of the Imam, who has become the Imam. The son of the Imam who has become the Imam, his *qalb al-haykal* is formed by this, who? The *Bab al-Abwab*. That is, in the *haykal* that the son of the Imam has, the highest form in it is of that *Bab al-Abwab*, who performed a great service. He is the greatest servant of the Imam, for he gathered such a large gathering through arrangements. Yes. Now what is being said here? *La hawla wa la quwwata illa billah*. There is no power and no strength except in Allah, by His grace. So who is meant by Allah? He is the Imam of the era and time. *'Aliyy al-'Azim*, who are they? They are the *ayn al-a'immah* (essence of the Imams) who are the manifestation of 'Ali, 'alayhis salam. So all the A'immah are the *hijabs* (veils) of *'Aliyy al-'Azim*. Who is meant by *'Aliyy al-'Azim*? *Ayn al-a'immah*. All the A'immah are the *hijabs* of *Ayn al-a'immah*. So in this, the entire picture comes, from the believer to *'ayn al-a'immah*, the process that is happening.

Ya Allahumma, ashrah al-fatihah hadiyyatan wasilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan mardiyyatan ila ruhi Sayyidina 'Ali ibn Hibatullah, qaddasa Allahu ruhahu wa anwara darihahu wa da'afa 'alayna min barakatihi wa ta'idatihi wa 'inayatihi wa taqabbal minna al-ifadah wa al-istifadah bi-haqqi Sayyidina Muhammadin wa 'alihi wa barik wa sallim. Al-Fatihah.

<!-- lecture 10 -->

A'udhu billahi minash shaitanir rajim. Bi fadli Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Wa bihi nasta'in wa ṣalla'llāhu 'alā rasūlihi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa 'alā āli Muhammadin wa bārik wa sallim.

Al-su'āl al-thāmin 'an al-jawāhir wa'l-yawāqīt (The eighth question, concerning jewels and rubies). And the eighth question is related to jewels and ruby, the stone, it is related to that.

Now, see, everything has a `ghāyah` (ultimate point). Meaning, its limit. So, `ghāyat al-ahjār` (the ultimate point of stones), `ghāyat al-ma'dan` (the ultimate point of minerals) — that the minerals which are inside the earth, or the stones, what is their `ghāyah`? Meaning, the most noble thing among them. So that is the ruby (`Yaqut`). All the jewels that there are, and all the minerals, and all the stones (`ahjār`), their `ghāyah` is the ruby.

In the same way, `ghāyat al-ḥaywān` (the ultimate point of animals) — all the animals that there are, their `ghāyah` is the elephant (`fīl`). So it was said that its reason would be its intelligence. Because the elephant is very intelligent among animals. So, due to its intelligence, it becomes the `ghāyah`.

In the same way, `ghāyat al-insān` (the ultimate point of man) is the `mu'min` (believer). The `ghāyah` of man is the `mu'min`. See the high station of the `mu'min`, that the single word `mu'min` — Allah Ta'ala has said it for us believers too, has included us in the rank of believers, those who are being honored with `Ahl al-Jalis`. And then, wherever in the glorious Quran `Ya ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū` (O you who believe) has come, its intention is Amir al-Mu'minin, and their leader, the leader of the believers, the `amīr`.

And Allah Ta'ala Himself has called Himself `Mu'min`. Among the names of Allah, one name is `Mu'min`, `al-Mu'min`. So, there are ranks. Yes. So, this is the condition. And `awwalu man āmana` (The first who believed) — the two verses of Surat al-Baqarah, the final verses, are extremely noble. And the Rasul says that one should recite it daily before sleeping. Because when the Rasulullah sallallahu alayhi wa aalihi wasallam went on the Mi'raj, two things were bestowed upon you. Allah bestowed two things. So you said that before this, before me, no prophet was bestowed these two things. And what were those things? One was Surat al-Fatiha, which was bestowed upon you. And the second was the last two verses of Surat al-Baqarah. Yes, these were bestowed. Although we, in `ṣadaqa'llāh`, recite the last three verses. But the mention is of the last two verses. So in it, the phrase `awwalu man āmana` has been used. So the first who believed... so you see, the numerical value you will derive for `awwalu man āmana` is the numerical value of Ali ibn Abi Talib. Where all are its interpretations found for Janab Ali? Look. So the numerical value of `awwalu man āmana` and the numerical value of `Ali ibn Abi Talib` are equal. Yes.

So, concerning the jewels (`jawāhir`) and rubies (`yawāqīt`), a question was asked: `hal hiya tashtami'u ilā maḥallin ma'rūf wa makānin mawṣūf` (Do they gather in a known place and a described location?). Does their gathering happen at a special place, or a place that is famous and has been described? `Wa tanḥallu wa tanqasimu 'ala'l-kawākib` (And do they dissolve and get distributed upon the stars/planets?). Or do they get ground up and then distributed among the `kawākib`? `Wa idhā kāna yashtami'u fa-'alā kam muddatuhā` (And if it gathers, then for what duration?). If its gathering is happening in the `kawākib`, then for what duration does it gather? `Wa kayfa intiqā'uhā` (And how is its selection?). And in the `kawākib`... its gathering is happening, so when will the selection of the `kawākib` be? `Hal hiya ta'ūdu ilā mawḍi'ihā'l-qadīm` (Does it return to its ancient place?). Or will these jewels and rubies then return to their original state? After being ground and going into the `kawākib`, will their gathering happen in their original state again? So the questioner has asked this. `Tafaḍḍalū 'alayya` (Please favor me). So, favor me because you are worthy of it. `Fa'l-'abdu yas'alu mālikahu min faḍlihi` (For the servant asks of his master from his bounty). And the servant asks his master concerning his bounty.

`Al-jawāb 'alā dhālika's-su'āl` (The answer to that question). The answer to that question: `naqūlu bi-'awni'llāhi'l-Kabīri'l-Muta'āl` (we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High). We say with the help of Allah, who is the Great, the Most High. He is most exalted. `Wa mannat Waliyyi'l-'Aṣri wa'z-Zamān 'alayhi ṣalawātu'r-Raḥīmi'r-Raḥmān` (And by the grace of the Master of the Era and the Time, upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). And by the grace, meaning by the gift, of the one who is the `Wali al-'Asr wa'l-Zamān`, may the blessings of Al-Rahim and Al-Rahman be established upon you. And may there be `ṣalawāt` upon you. And by the aid (`tā'īd`) of `Mawlāyī al-'Alam, 'Alam al-Hudā`. And by the aids of Sayyidna Hibatullah Saheb, who are very luminous. `Wa sirruhu'l-bāhir al-sārī ilā laṭā'ifi'ṣ-ṣuwar` (And his manifest secret, flowing to the subtleties of the forms). And from your manifest secret, which is flowing and جاری towards the subtleties of the forms, `anna'l-yawāqīt wa'l-jawāher al-ḥisān tuḥfaẓu fī khazā'ini'l-mulūk` (that the fine rubies and jewels are preserved in the treasuries of kings). And the jewels and rubies, which are fine, they... their protection is done in the treasuries of kings. `Wa tuṣān` (And they are protected). And there, its complete protection is done. `Thumma tansha'u matā shā'a'l-mudabbir` (Then it arises whenever the Disposer wills). And they remain for a period. In crowns... they are set in crowns and are at places of honor. And then, until the Disposer wills, they begin to be ground. They come into a state where they are ground up. `Wa yasīru fī'l-amākini'sh-sharīfah` (And it travels in the noble places). And then they... the noble places... for example, as it has been told, or other than that, from the exquisite places, which are noble and exalted places, their travel happens there. `Wa matā abala'l-aflāk wa'l-kawākib sārat hādhihi li-taghliba fī'l-khidmah` (And when the celestial spheres and planets wore out, these traveled to prevail in service). And when the `aflāk` and `kawākib` fall, when each one has to ascend towards the spiritual realm, so when does its ascent happen? When... every thing that ascends and makes an ascent, it has to touch the earth. Except for whom? For the `Falak al-Muḥīṭ` (the encompassing sphere). The `Falak al-Muḥīṭ`, right from where it is, becomes the Universal Intellect (`'Aql al-Kullī`) and enters the spiritual realm. Yes. But all things, whether it is the moon, the sun, or the `aflāk`, all of them have to touch the earth, and after that, their progress happens. Yes.

[Side-discussion begins]
Yes, well, what falls from above, meteorites fall, they come to the earth, right? They are very valuable. Yes, they become very valuable. So all of this is the same thing. Probably, yes, it is.
The mention of the elephant that has come, its physical strength is also very great. In the elephant... there is, but due to its length and breadth, and its shrewdness, it has been given honor.
The horse is also there.
Yes, the horse is in its own place. But what has been said is that in the elephant, the elephant has more shrewdness than the horse.
[Side-discussion ends]

So these `aflāk` and `kawākib` ascend... when they fall, they ascend. This... what thing? The jewels and the rubies, they take their place (`khilāfat`) in service. `Bi-ḥikmatin muḥarriki'l-'awālim` (By the wisdom of the Mover of the worlds). With a wisdom that is the Mover of all things. `Wa baqiyat muddata'l-kawni'l-ādamī` (And it remained for the duration of the Adamite existence). And for a duration, for the duration of a `kawr` of Adam, they remain. Yes. Meaning, `khilāfat`s are established. When the `aflāk` and `kawākib` progress, who takes their `khilāfat`? These... jewels and rubies take it. And for one `kawr` of Adam, their duration remains. `Ilā an tawaffā mā 'alayhā min al-khidam` (Until it fulfilled the service that was upon it). And until their services are completed... `Wa yajrī'l-amru 'alā hādhihi'l-khuṭiyyah ḥattā yaṣ'ada'l-'ālamu bi'l-kulliyyah` (And the matter proceeds along this path until the world ascends in its entirety). And in this way, the matter keeps going on until the entire world ascends. And when does that happen? At the end of a `kawr`. `Li-qawli aṣdaqi'l-qā'ilīn` (As per the saying of the most truthful of speakers). As `aṣdaq al-qā'ilīn`, meaning Allah Ta'ala says, `kamā bada'nā awwala khalqin nu'īduhu` (As We began the first creation, We will repeat it). Just as We had begun the first creation, `wa'dan 'alaynā` (a promise upon Us), We promise this matter, `innā kunnā fā'ilīn` (indeed, We are performers [of it]). And We will do it again. Yes. Meaning, this coming and going of the `kawr`, all these things will keep happening.

Then what comes next?
The ninth question.
The ninth question, yes... In Yasin, the last verse... it is also about this, that He will create again. What is the last verse? `ilayhi turja'ūn` (to Him you will be returned).
[unclear]
`Awa laysa'lladhī khalaqa's-samāwāti wa'l-arḍa bi-qādirin 'alā an yakhluqa mithlahum, balā wa huwa'l-khallāqu'l-'alīm` (Is not He who created the heavens and the earth able to create the like of them? Yes, and He is the knowing Creator). That's what he's saying. The last one.
Is it not so that He who created the heavens and the earth is able to... yes... `mithlahum wa huwa'l-khallāqu'l-'alīm` (their like, and He is the knowing Creator). He is the `Khallāq al-'Alīm`. And did not Allah create the heavens and the earth? He is able, to create it again. Yes. The same system will be recreated.
Yes. After `kawkab`, a new system comes.

Al-su'āl al-tāsi' 'an ziyārat al-qubūr (The ninth question, concerning visiting graves). That the visitation that is done of graves, `kayfa dhālikā` (how is that?), `wa mā yaḥḍuru'l-qāṣidīn li'l-ajsmān` (and what attends the visitors to the bodies?). And those... the graves, and those who have been buried in the graves, the intention that is made to visit them, so when they are present, `kayfa shu'ūru'l-maqṣūd` (how is the feeling of the one intended?). So do they feel, the people whose graves are being visited, do they feel that the visitor is visiting them?

And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir... this was a very high-ranking elder, 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir. He was a `mubashshir al-nabī` (one who gave glad tidings of the prophet). He was from before the prophet. 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir was a `mubashshir al-nabī`. He is from before the Prophet alayhis salam, his birth happened and his death. And he gave the glad tidings of the coming of the Huzur. And his death happened in Najran. And at `Ukhdūd` his death happened. For which, in Surah Buruj, isn't there? In Surah Buruj, `Ukhdūd` is mentioned. So at the place of `Ukhdūd`, his death happened. In that era, we have seen his grave and all. So at that time, it wasn't cared for. It was kept in such a poor condition. There was just a mound. But later, I saw in pictures that it has been done very well. Yes. And we... when we used to go on `da'wat`s in the time of Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan, so when we had to pass from here, from `Ukhdūd`, Sayyidna Husayn bin Hasan would definitely recite `irādah` (a prayer) for him. He would recite a du'a, recite an `irādah`.

So, `wa qad ṣāra lahu hādhihi'l-muddah al-madīdah` (and for him this long period has passed). And a long period has passed. The questioner says. And I have started asking a lot of questions to you. And I have gone beyond my means. But my state is such that I am very troubled. So the questioner keeps asking questions. And you are from the people of favor and bounty (`ahl al-faḍl wa'l-nawāl`). You are people of favor and givers of gifts.

`Al-jawāb 'alā dhālika's-su'āl` (The answer to that question). Your question... my answer to it is that `naqūlu bi-'awni'llāhi'l-Kabīri'l-Muta'āl` (we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Most High). We say with the help of Allah, who is the Great, the Most High. `Wa mannat Waliyyi'l-'Aṣri wa'z-Zamān` (And by the grace of the Master of the Era and the Time). And by the gift of the Master of the Era and the Time, `alayhi ṣalawātu'r-Raḥīmi'r-Raḥmān` (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). Upon you be the blessings of Al-Rahim and Al-Rahman. `Wa tā'īdi Mawlāyī al-'Alam` (And by the aid of my Master, the distinguished one). By the aid of my Master, Maulana Sayyidna Hibatullah. `Wa sirruhu'l-bāhir bi-nūri'n-naẓm` (And his manifest secret with the light of the order). And from your manifest secret, `anna ziyārat al-qubūr sirrun mastūr` (that the visitation of graves is a concealed secret), `wa huwa'l-muttaṣil bi-dhālika'l-ḥaddi 'ala'l-ghurūr` (and it is connected to that rank over deception). And it is that the visitation of graves... the concealed secret is that the one who is visiting is receiving emanations (`faṣūl`) from the rank, which is a high rank whose visitation is being done. `Li-yashfa'a lahu 'inda jāmi'i'ṣ-ṣuwar` (So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms). So that he may intercede for him in the gathering of the `ṣuwar`, meaning in those `ṣuwar` where he is present, from there he intercedes for him. The higher the rank, their spiritual powers also increase. In life, they were angels `bi'l-quwwah` (in potential). Meaning, the `hudūd` of the Dawah were angels, but they were angels `bi'l-quwwah`. But after death, they become angels `bi'l-fi'l` (in actuality). They become angels `bi'l-fi'l`. And it has also come in another place that when a visitor visits, they feel it, that the visitor is visiting. And their... as has been said here, their luminous form (`ṣūrat nayyirah`), whose? The one being visited, whose visitation is being done, their luminous form is always looking towards its `fuḍalā'` (spiritual particle). Just as we... towards our own things, wherever we may be sitting, but our mind is on our house and our things that are in our house. We... even if we are not in the house, and are not near our things, despite that, our thoughts are towards it, towards our valuable things. In the same way, the one who has passed away, their form also... what are its valuable things? It is their `fuḍalā'` which is progressing. So it wants that as soon as possible, its `fuḍalā'` should progress and become... go into the `aflāk` and progress. So it wants this. Therefore, with its luminous form, which is in the `hiyākil` (spiritual structures) or is with the `hudūd 'āliyah` (higher ranks), which is going to reach the `Bāb al-Abwāb` (Gate of Gates), so from there, it exerts an `ishrāq` (illumination). Its illuminations are always flowing. And in that circle of `ishrāq`, when a `mu'min` comes, then that... he also gets benefit. And he intercedes for him. And as Huzur, the Master of the Universe, said that when children go to their parents, to the grave, then they, the parents, feel it that the children have come to visit them. And they are as happy as they were when they were small and would come and sit in their laps, the parents', and how much joy the parents would feel. So, they feel that same joy, the mother and father. This is the saying of the Rasul. That the Rasul said that parents, upon the visitation of their children, become extremely happy and they feel pleasure. And when the children pray for them and recite Yasin and so on, or perform `naẓr` and `niyāz`, then they feel this matter. Yes. And until... which is in Surah Bani Isra'il, `Rabbirḥamhumā kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīrā` (My Lord, have mercy upon them as they brought me up [when I was] small). Often people read it incorrectly, they read `rabbiyānī`, but it is `rabbayānī`. It is `rabbayānī`, not `rabbiyānī`. So, `kamā rabbayānī ṣaghīrā`, so, O Allah, have mercy on them, just as when I was small, they had mercy on me. So this `du'ā`, one should recite it in their lifetime too, and after their death. Mercy... mercy is not that only after death you have mercy. If they are present in life, then the children should pray for them too, for the mother and father, they should make this Quranic `du'ā`, that O Allah, have mercy, as they had mercy on me when I was small. So have mercy on them.

[Side-discussion begins]
If the visitor is of a higher rank, then the matter is reversed. Yes, meaning, it happens that if the visitor is higher, then from him, the aids and the help are received by the lower person. Just as water always flows from a higher surface to a lower surface.
The grave of a great elder, a prophet, for them there are holy traces. Their fuḍalā' is not there, I know. But there are traces. Its trace would be something?
There are traces, no. There are traces. Even if there is no `fuḍalā'`, but the `āthār` remain. And it is because of the `āthār` that the blessings of the grave remain.
Just as from a bottle of `'iṭr` (perfume), even after the `'iṭr` has left, the fragrance remains in the bottle of `'iṭr`. So in the same way, an analogy has been given that from the grave, despite the progress of the `fuḍalā'`, its `āthār` remain. Yes. The place of the grave itself will become the place of his `ayndah futteh`.
Yes, yes.
[Side-discussion ends]

`Wa li-yashfa'a lahu 'inda majāmi'i'ṣ-ṣuwar, wa li-yakshifa 'anhu mā 'arāhu min funūni'l-maṣā'ib` (So that he may intercede for him at the gathering of the forms, and so that he may remove from him the various types of calamities that have afflicted him). To the extent that the various calamities that are befalling the visitor, they remove those calamities too. Just as we have this concept that we go to the graves of the `awliyā' Allāh` (saints of Allah) and say, "You, Allah is the one who will help, but you have become the means (`wasīlah`) that through your means, Allah Ta'ala may hear our prayers and help us." But this `wasīlah` is also a very high thing, as it is in the Quran: `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` (And seek the means of approach to Him). And generally, the people of the common folk (`'awām al-nās`), the misguided people, they mislead in such a way that they... the concept we have of `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah`, that reach Him through the means, meaning through the Ahl al-Bayt. But what do they have? They call it the glorious Quran. `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` means reach Allah through the Quran. This is not understood. `Wa'btaghū ilayhi'l-wasīlah` means reach Allah through the means, meaning through the Rasul and the Ahl al-Bayt, or the followers of the Ahl al-Bayt who are the `du'āt`, `ḥujjat`, and `imām`, through them, you reach Allah. Because the `ma'rifat` (gnosis) of the Worshipped (`ma'būd`) is the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd` (ranks of the Dawah). The `ma'rifat` of the `ma'būd` is the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd`. The `ma'rifat` of the Worshipped, of Allah, is the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd`. Within the `ma'rifat` of the `hudūd` is the `ma'rifat` of Allah.

`Wa mā 'alima bihi min jamī'i'l-ma'āqib` (And what he knew of all the consequences). And whatever affliction is befalling him from various matters, `li-kawnihi yushrifu 'alā fuḍalatihi` (because he is overseeing his `fuḍalā'`). Because that person is exerting an `ishrāq` (illumination) on his `fuḍalā'`. The one whose grave is being visited, their `ṣūrat sharīf` is exerting an `ishrāq` on their `fuḍalā'`. `Wa yaḥummu 'alayhā` (And he cares for it). And upon it... he is very merciful. Yes, on his `fuḍalā'`. `Wa yudabbiruhā` (And he manages it). And he is managing it. How to make the `fuḍalā'` advance as quickly as possible. `Wa yaḥḍuru'l-qāṣidīna ilayhā` (And the visitors attend to it). And its... the visitors are present towards its `fuḍalā'`. So these things are then obtained by him, by the visitors, because his `ishrāq` is happening towards his `fuḍalā'`, so in that circle, these visitors come. And this is also there that... the lowest `ishrāq` that happens... the higher `ishrāq`s do happen... what is the lowest `ishrāq` happening? The lowest `ishrāq` that happens for a `mu'min` is the `ishrāq` of his `ṣūrat sharīf` on his body. Although his `ṣūrat sharīf` is not yet formed. Rather, for him, it is still the `nafs nāmīyah` (vegetative soul), `nafs ḥissīyah` (sensory soul), `nafs nāṭiqah` (rational soul) form. But the `nafs nāṭiqah`, because it is a `ṣūrat sharīf` `bi'l-quwwah` (in potential), its `ishrāq` is happening, its own `nafs` is exerting `ishrāq` on its body. This is the lowest `ishrāq` which is happening. After that, the higher `ishrāq`s of the Imam, etc., yes. Because he has been placed in Surat al-Fatiha. How many verses does Surat al-Fatiha have? Seven verses. But its words (`kalimāt`) are 25. The words of Surat al-Fatiha are 25. And the 25 words... in the glorious Quran, only 25 prophets have been mentioned. Although there are 124,000 prophets, but Allah Ta'ala has mentioned only 25 prophets. And in Surat al-Fatiha too, there are 25 words. And the 25 words indicate 12 `hudūd` of the `jazā'ir` (islands) and 12 `hudūd` of the `ḥaḍarāt` (presences). Meaning, the `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir`, who are 12, and the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, who are 12. And their leader, the leader of these 24, is the Imam of the Time. In this way, it becomes 25. Yes. And these 12 `hudūd` of the `jazā'ir`, or `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir`, they are at their own places. They are carrying out their `da'wat`, in their own language and at their own places they are carrying out the `da'wat`. But the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, because the manifestation of the Imam of the Time is there, so their `da'wat` is a collective `da'wat`. Whereas here, in the `jazā'ir`, it is an individual `da'wat`. There, it is not an individual `da'wat`, but a collective `da'wat`. Therefore, when we pray the `tahajjud` prayer, we pray these two-two `rak'at` prayers, or perform six `salām`s, because these `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir`, their connections are in pairs of two. One `dā'ī` is there, so he has a companion. Another `dā'ī` is there, so he has a companion. In this way. But the `du'āt` of the `jazā'ir`... `ḥaḍarāt`... the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, and... so they are carrying out the `da'wat` collectively. And their example is what? What we pray, `ṣalāt al-layl`. In the `ṣalāt al-layl` prayer we pray, we pray the 12 `rak'at`s consecutively. We pray them all at once. And after that, there is no `du'ā`, look. After the `ṣalāt al-layl` prayer, there is no `du'ā`, right?
There is, there is.
There is a du'a, a very big du'a. A very long du'a.
Yes, the du'a is very big.
After any other prayer there is no du'a.
Well... `ṣalāt al-layl` is the final prayer. The final prayer before `shaf'a` is the final prayer. And it indicates the `du'āt` of the `ḥaḍarāt`, it indicates them. They are very high. They are very high-ranking `du'āt`. Because from these very `du'āt`, from their `fuḍalā'`, from their `riyāḍ`, the `Bāb al-Salsal` is formed. And the `Bāb al-Anbiyā'` are formed. So these are people of a very high rank. Yes.

[Side-discussion begins]
The conscience, right... `zamīr`. My conscience does not allow it. So I feel that my conscience is saying... No, it is the guidance of the `nāṭiqah`. The `nāṭiqah` always wants to keep its `nāmīyah` and `ḥissīyah` with it. And for that, it strives. The stronger the `nāṭiqah`, it will exert `ishrāq` on its `nāmīyah`, `ḥissīyah`, and on the body's... on the body, and it will try to elevate it. Therefore, some `hudūd` of the Dawah have also been such that... what happens is that after death, the `nāmīyah`, `ḥissīyah`, and `nāṭiqah` join together and become the `ṣūrat sharīf`. But some `hudūd` of the Dawah who are pious and God-fearing, extremely so, for them it happens that not only the `nāmīyah`, `ḥissīyah`, and `nāṭiqah`, but also their liver's parts and their lower body parts also ascend with them. Due to their nobility and the life of piety and purity they lived, their... yes.
But my... conscience... everyone has a conscience, right? But everyone has taken an oath. Some are bound by the oath, some are not. So consciously I... I say to my conscience, "No, I have to do this wrong thing." So I am doing it with intention, the wrong act or the right act, I am doing it with intention. Yes. So, it's not like because you are from the Ahl al-Silsilah, you will walk on the straight path. You have free will (`ikhtiyār`).
[Side-discussion ends]

Free will is there. But you see, this free will is a very great thing, which nature has given only to man. Angels have not been given free will. Because angels cannot sin. Right? What is the verse in the Quran? That `walā ya'ṣūn` (and they do not disobey). That they do not sin, the angels. And for a human... and see, that Jannah and Dozakh (paradise and hell), for whom have they been made? They have been made only for humans. Although, among Allah's creations there are many. There is the moon, the sun, the `aflāk`, right? There is the earth, and what things are not there? There are animals, there are minerals, and all things are present. But Jannah and Dozakh are only for humans. Because man is the only creature who has the choice (`ikhtiyār`) to do good and to do evil. Although, for him, goodness is clear, what a good deed is. And evil is clear for him, what an evil deed is. Despite that, knowing goodness, he is inclined towards evil. And knowing evil, he is inclined towards goodness. He has the choice, complete choice. So because of his choice, Jannah and Dozakh have been... if he does good, he will be near Jannah. If he does evil, he will be near Dozakh. So this condition is only for man. It is not for others. That is why...
For the one with `nāṭiqah`, with `juzur`.
Yes, but among Allah's creations there are many, right? It is only man, for whom Jannah and Dozakh have been created. And there is a `du'ā` in which they say... the author of the `du'ā` says that you should ask for Jannah five times daily. And five times, seek refuge from Dozakh. `Allāhumma innī...` what is its tone? `Allāhumma innī as'aluka'l-jannah... Allāhumma innī as'aluka'l-jannah... As'alu'llāha'l-jannah` (O Allah, I ask You for paradise... I ask Allah for paradise). I ask Allah to give me Jannah. `As'alu'llāha'l-jannah`. I ask Allah for Jannah. `Wa a'ūdhu bi'llāhi min al-nār` (And I seek refuge in Allah from the Fire). And I pray to Allah that I may be protected from Dozakh. So it has come to do this five times. Yes.

[Side-discussion begins]
We use a slightly different tone. The du'a after the `adhan`. Yes, `rabbanā ātinā...` yes. That is in its own place. But here, we are asking Allah to grant us Jannah. Its tone has become different. That du'a is in its own place. It is the du'a of Amir al-Mu'minin, in fact. What we do after the `adhan`. Five times... five times. Some do it five times.
At different times.
And this is... together. Five times to seek refuge, and five times to ask.
[Side-discussion ends]

Yes. And that du'a after the `adhan`, because between the `adhan` and `iqāmah`, `du'ā`s are accepted. The five times when `du'ā`s are accepted, one of them is the time between the `adhan` and the `iqāmah`. In it, `du'ā`s are accepted. So we pray, as Amir al-Mu'minin said, `Allāhumma innī as'aluka'l-jannah... wa an tuzawwajanī min al-ḥūri'l-'īn` (O Allah, I ask You for paradise... and that You marry me to the `ḥūr 'īn`). So who are the `ḥūr 'īn`? They are actually your `hudūd 'āliyah`. That my unification, my unification, may You grant it with the `hudūd 'āliyah`. Yes.

`Wa 'an Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir` (And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir). And concerning Sayyidna 'Abdullah ibn Thāmir, the question you have asked, `wa annahu lahu hādhihi'l-muddah` (and that for him has been this duration). For him, a period has passed. `Wa anna qad zāda 'ala'l-alfi fi'l-'iddah` (And that it has exceeded a thousand in count). For him, a period of 1000 years has passed. A period of more than 1000 years has passed. `Wa in kāna dhālika wa qad ṣa'ida'l-fuḍalā'` (And if that is so, the `fuḍalā'` has indeed ascended). While his `fuḍalā'` has progressed after 1000 years, all of it (`jamī'uhā`), its flesh (`laḥmuhā`), its bones (`'aẓmuhā`), and its nerves (`'aṣabuhā`), all have progressed. `Fa-'āda'l-āthār` (So the traces returned). But those `āthār` (traces) remain. In that grave, the `āthār` remain. `Wa't-turbatu'sh-sharīfah allatī'jtajanat bihā` (And the noble earth in which it was enveloped). And the noble soil, such that with it, with their `fuḍalā'`, it had been mixed. `Wa ishrāqu'ṣ-ṣuwar fī tilka'l-amākini'ṭ-ṭahārah` (And the illumination of the forms in those places of purity). And upon you, the `ishrāq` that is happening, towards your `fuḍalā'`, and towards its `āthār`, and towards that soil, in which soil your body was hidden, towards that, the `ishrāq` of the `ṣuwar` is with it. In that soil as well, nobility was created, right? So, `wa alā qadri'z-zā'ir wa'l-mazūr tuqābiluhu'z-zubadu'ẓ-ẓāhirah` (and according to the rank of the visitor and the visited, the manifest quintessences meet him). That the noble places that were there, and according to the rank of the visitor and the visited, they are met by the `zubad`. `Zubad` are what? The `fuḍalā'`, after progressing, has to go to Baytullah. Our great grave, from the small grave is the great grave. The small grave is the one in which we are placed after dying. And the great grave is Baytullah. So, to go to Baytullah, there are 28 stages (`manāzil`). One has to cross 28 stages. Meaning, its stages... from where he has passed away, from there, slowly, he comes to his place in the masjid where he used to pray. And what is the habit of one who prays? He makes a special place for himself. He goes and prays there. So what does that become for him? It becomes the place for the gathering of his `fuḍalā'`. Then until he progresses, he comes to his `mihrab` (prayer niche). He comes to the `mihrab`. To the extent that from the `mihrab` he progresses, he comes to the shrine of Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan. His `fuḍalā'`. And...

So, the progression of the `fuḍalā'` is happening. So the 28 stages are in this manner, that Sayyidna Sulayman bin Hasan's shrine it came to, then from there it left and progressed. It went to various noble places (`amākin sharīfah`). To the extent that it goes to the `mawāqīt` (appointed places). `Mawāqīt` meaning the five `mawāqīt` from where they tie the intention for `umrah`, it comes there too. That is why it is said that there, `fuḍalāt` and `riyāḍ` gather there. To the extent that after progressing, from Anderganj Charkhan, it comes to Harame Baytullah. The `fuḍalā'` of it. To the extent that it comes to various `rukn`s (corners). To the extent that it comes to the `rukn al-aswad` (black stone corner) of Baytullah. The `fuḍalā'`. And this process is جاری. It is happening continuously. To the extent that it ascends from there. And the state of ascending is called `zubad`. When it ascends, that `fuḍalā'`, in the state of `fuḍalā'`, it is. But when it... when they come to the pillars of Baytullah, they are called `zakhā'ir` (treasures). And when they ascend, where do they ascend? They ascend to the `aflāk` (celestial spheres). When they ascend to the `aflāk`, they are called `zubad`. And `zubad` is a very high state of being. Yes.

So the `zubad sharīfah`, they meet them. The visitor and the one whose grave is being visited. And they also help them. Yes. `Wa aydan fa-inna kullu qabrin ḍamma hādhihi'l-ḥudūd` (And also, that every grave that contains these `hudūd`). And it is that every grave is connected to these `hudūd mu'aẓẓamah`. `Fa-taḥtahu sirrun 'aẓīm min al-khamā'iri'l-kāminah` (For beneath it is a great secret from the latent leavens). And beneath it, meaning beneath the grave, is a great secret from the hidden leavens. `Fī tilka'l-biqā'i'l-mukarramah` (In those honored spots). Which are their noble places. `Fa-li-dhālika wajabat ziyāratuhā` (Therefore, their visitation became obligatory). Because of this... the hidden leavens that are there, and the noble things that are exerting `ishrāq` on them, the `ṣuwar` are exerting `ishrāq`, on the grave, and things have progressed, `āthār` have remained. So all these things that are there, so because of that what is happening? Their visitation becomes obligatory, that they should be visited. Because those places are very pure and clean, and they benefit the `mu'min`. They benefit the visitor. As a story happened, in the time of Wali al-Muqaddas, a person named Amanullah Saheb, who was a `Mulla Adib`. And the village called Taliwala, he was the `mulla` there. And it so happened that in his final days, he got cancer. And the cancer was of his jaw. And his condition was very bad. He was shown to various doctors, but that cancer kept increasing. He was a man of very firm faith. He had great faith in Huzur. So what would he do? Taliwala and Khankhah Sharif are very close to each other. The closest village is this one, Khankhah Sharif's village. At Khankhah Sharif, there are two elders of ours. Sayyidi Yusuf bin Ali Khan and Sayyidi Sharif Muhammad Saheb Sindhi. Both. So he would go there and sit as if in `i'tikāf` (seclusion). And the soil of that grave, he would apply it to that place. So this was his routine. This routine continued for months. And then it happened that he went and showed it to the doctors later, and a disease like cancer was gone, and he was completely fine. Yes. So this condition also happens, that these masters of the graves, who have lived a noble life, so in their presence, they were `bi'l-quwwah` (in potential), and now they have become `bi'l-fi'l` (in actuality). And because of which... because of becoming `bi'l-fi'l`, they have the choice to benefit their followers and those who have faith in them. In this way, benefit is received.

Amanullah who... the hospital, their son... yes, yes. The one who has just built the hospital, their father. Salim who has built the hospital, their father was he, Amanullah Saheb. Yes.

`Allatī lā ḥādatah` (Which has no limit). Such that they... those noble `ṣuwar` meet them. They meet the visitor and the one whose grave is being visited. Their `ṣuwar` are always looking towards their `fuḍalā'`. And because of that, `ishrāq` keeps happening there. The `ishrāq` of the `ṣūrah` happens towards its `fuḍalā'`. Therefore, in that circle, the person who comes to visit, he also gets benefit. And he also benefits.

As you have mentioned, in Imam Husayn's, the soil of the grave is considered `khāk-e-shifā'` (soil of healing). Yes, absolutely. And it is also a saying of Huzur, the Master of the Universe, `wa idhā ḍāqati'ṣ-ṣudūr fa'sta'īnū bi-ahli'l-qubūr` (and when the chests become constricted, then seek help from the people of the graves). When your chests become constricted, meaning you find no way out, you are in such trouble that you do not understand how to remove your trouble, so `wa idhā ḍāqati'ṣ-ṣudūr`, your chests become constricted, then `fa'sta'īnū bi-ahli'l-qubūr` (then seek help from the people of the graves). Then you seek help from the people of the graves. It was not said that seek help from Allah. Allah is the one who will help, in the end. But these are people of Allah, so because they are people of Allah, Allah helps them. And because of that, you... you receive help. Yes. As it is in the Quran, that Janab Isa's story, for Isa alayhis salam, the verses that were revealed, so in them what was said? That... `man anṣārī ilā'llāh` (Who are my helpers for Allah?). Right? `Qāla'l-ḥawāriyyūna naḥnu anṣāru'llāh` (The disciples said, 'We are the helpers of Allah'). "For the sake of Allah, who becomes my helper?" So the disciples said, `naḥnu anṣāru'llāh` (We are the helpers of Allah). "We are the helpers of Allah." So does Allah need help? This is a Quranic verse. The verse has come. `Naḥnu anṣāru'llāh`. "We are the helpers of Allah."

`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Allāhumma ashrik fi'l-fātiḥah, hadiyyatan waṣilatan suniyyatan zakiyyatan naḍiyyah ilā rūḥ Sayyidna Ali bin Hibatullah, qaddasa'llāhu rūḥahu wa nawwara ḍarīḥah, wa ḍā'afa 'alaynā min barakātihi wa lahdhatihi wa tā'īdātihi, wa taqabbal minnā al-ifādah wa'l-istifādah fī a'ẓam mā yakūnu bi-ḥaqqi Sayyidna Muhammadin wa ālihi ajma'īn wa sallim al-Fātiḥah.`
`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-ḥamdu li'llāhi Rabbi'l-'ālamīn, al-Raḥmāni'r-Raḥīm, Māliki yawmi'd-dīn, iyyāka na'budu wa iyyāka nasta'īn, ihdinā'ṣ-ṣirāṭa'l-mustaqīm, ṣirāṭa'lladhīna an'amta 'alayhim ghayri'l-maghḍūbi 'alayhim wa lā'ḍ-ḍāllīn.`
[unclear recitation]
`Al-Fātiḥah.`
`Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-ḥamdu li'llāhi Rabbi'l-'ālamīn, al-Raḥmāni'r-Raḥīm, Māliki yawmi'd-dīn, iyyāka na'budu wa iyyāka nasta'īn, ihdinā'ṣ-ṣirāṭa'l-mustaqīm, ṣirāṭa'lladhīna an'amta 'alayhim ghayri'l-maghḍūbi 'alayhim wa lā'ḍ-ḍāllīn.`

<!-- lecture 11 -->

As-salamu alaykum. Wa alaykum as-salam.

Our… this book's, from *Kunooz al Hikmah*, the tenth question that is there… *as-su'al al-'ashir* (the tenth question). After that, what is the word? *Al-hadi*. *Dhibh al-huda*. *Dhibh al-huda*. *Dhibh al-huda*. *Dhibh al-huda*. The *dhibh* (slaughter) of the *hadi*. *Hadi*. *Dhibh al-hadi fi Mina*. So, *ma al-haqiqatuhu* (what is its reality)? *Ma al-haqiqatuhu*.

This… one is a *hadiyah*, which comes in the meaning of a gift. And *al-hadi* means… that is what sacrifice is called. Sacrifice is called *hadi*. So… and then, *Muna* is also… we also say *Mina*, and we also say *Muna*. But *Muna* is much better than *Mina*. *Muna* means desire, the ultimate desire is called *Muna*. So, the *zabihat* (slaughter) that takes place in Mina of an animal, which a *haji* (pilgrim) is slaughtering, what is its reality? The questioner has asked this question. *Wa amma al-mathal wal-mawsul* (and as for the metaphor and its referent). And what is its *mathal* (metaphor) and *mawsul* (referent)? *Faqad 'arraftuhu min tafaddulukum* (For I have known it from your favor). And, I have become aware of it… from it, from your favor, of what its *mathal* and *mawsul* is.

*Wa ma yakunu min Jabal 'Arafat* (And what is from Mount 'Arafat). And *Jabal 'Arafat*… that is, in the plain of 'Arafat, there is *Jabal 'Arafat*, which… that which is the highest part of 'Arafat, it… *faqad 'araftu al-mathal* (for I have known the metaphor). I have understood its apparent meaning. *Wa tafaddalu bil-jawab al-shafi wal-manwal al-kafi* (So please favor [me] with the healing answer and the sufficient provision). So, you must fully make me aware.

*Al-jawab 'ala dhalika as-su'al* (The answer to that question). *Naqulu bi 'awnillah* (We say with the help of Allah). And when the *Sarwar-e-Kainat* (Master of the Universe) had stood at *Jabal 'Arafat*… in *'Arafat*, because the entire plain of *'Arafat* is a *mawquf* (place of standing). So there, you climbed the hill and recited *du'as* (prayers). Where now… Muslims also generally go to that hill.

So, *al-jawab 'ala dhalika as-su'al, naqulu bi 'awnillah al-kabir al-muta'al* (The answer to that question, we say with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted). In the answer to your question, I say that with the help of Allah, the Great, the Exalted, *wa minnat wali al-'asr wa al-zaman* (and the favor of the Guardian of the Age and Time), and with the grace of the *Wali al-'Asr wa al-Zaman*, *'alayhi as-salawat al-rahim al-rahman* (upon him be the blessings of the Merciful, the Compassionate). Upon him be the *salawat* (blessings) of the Merciful and the Compassionate. *Wa ta'id mawlai al-a'lam wa sirruhu al-bahir al-mu'azzam* (And the support of my master, the most knowledgeable, and his brilliant, magnificent secret). And from the support of *Mawlai al-A'lam*, that is, Hibatullah Saheb, and his brilliant secret that is, which is magnificent, from its secret, I am giving the answer. That *'an dhibh al-hadi fi Mina* (about the slaughter of the *hadi* in Mina). That the slaughter which is done in Mina, *wa ma al-haqiqah fi dhalika* (and what is the reality in that)? What is its reality?

Its meaning is… for that is when the *Mudabbir al-Hakim* (the Wise Planner) intends *min irqa'iha wa is'adiha 'ala an-nasl al-qawim* (from its elevation and its ascension upon the upright lineage), that the meat of that sacrifice be elevated and ascended to the pure lineage. And as for its souls… that is to say… it is a punishment for it. The slaughter is performed. And as for its *ruh* (soul), which is called *hissiya*, which is its *hissiya*, so, it… it is punished. By slaughtering it. From slaughtering it, it is punished, so that the *hissiya* leaves it… leaves that animal. Because if, within it… it remains, such as if an animal dies its own… its own death, so when it dies its own death, its *hissiya* goes into its body, and it is a bad form of death. That its *hissiya* has gone inside it. And we, when we perform the slaughter, taking the name of Allah, due to which the *hissiya* separates from it. Due to which we can eat its meat and can use its meat. So, therefore, the meat is elevated and ascended. And its… the *ruh* (soul) which is there, that is, the *hissiya*, it is punished and expelled.

The slaughter, apart from saying *Allahu Akbar*, if it is said in some other language, say, if *Allahu Akbar* is said, then will it be permissible? I don't think it's permissible. *Allahu Akbar* can be said, no, a person… No, for example, say, the Jews who perform the slaughter, *halal*… They don't say *Allahu Akbar*, they say it in their own language. Yes, but their slaughter is permissible (*ja'iz*). In that same regard… Yes. No, my meaning is that it should be for Muslims, that in another language… *Allahu Akbar*… No, a Muslim will say *Allahu Akbar*. But, for example, another form, like the Jews. Now, in their language, whatever they might say… Yes. So it is permissible. Both for the Jew and for the Christian, their slaughtered meat is *halal*.

And, sir, there where the goats, etc., were slaughtered, in Saudi Arabia, there was a butcher who used to do that work. I asked him how they slaughter there. They have put on a tape recording. *Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar* is on a tape, and… There, in the slaughterhouse. Yes. They do it with the slaughterhouse machine. Yes, I have also seen that.

It's not a correct method, is it? No, no. Now their contention is that how many times will we say it?

And from saying *Allahu Akbar*… how many great benefits there are. Look, there is a hadith of *Rasulullah Sarwar-e-Kainat* (the Messenger of God, Master of the Universe), that *as-salatu qurbanu kulli taqiyyin* (Prayer is the sacrifice of every pious person). Prayer is the sacrifice of every pious person. Here, you have used the word *qurban* (sacrifice). It is a sacrifice for this reason that we… when we perform a slaughter, we say *Allahu Akbar*, so that animal becomes *halal*. We can eat its meat. So we, in a state of prayer, after performing *wudu'* (ablution) and coming to the *masjid* (mosque), with the worship of Allah, we are saying *Allahu Akbar* over ourselves. So when it is our *nafs-e hissiya* (sensory soul), it too is being sacrificed over it. It is also being sacrificed. So, and its… the part which is there, whatever part comes out, just as a person is, so are his angels. So, its *nafs-e hissiya* is being expelled, and it is being struck continuously. Just as in a two-*rak'at* prayer, we use the word *Allahu Akbar* 11 times. So from *Allahu Akbar*… how magnificent… when one animal becomes *halal*, so we, in a state of *iman* (faith), are saying *Allahu Akbar* upon ourselves, so what a magnificent difference there must be. Because the *natiqa* (rational soul) of a believer is so secretive that it doesn't even let the *hissiya* (sensory soul), which is its closest soul, know its secrets. It doesn't inform the *namiya* (vegetative soul) or the *hissiya* about the things, the blessings, the divine effulgence it is receiving from *'Ali 'illiyyin*, it does not inform it in any way. This is the state of its *nafs-e natiqa*.

And you see that what does the Messenger say? That the most excellent *wazifa* (litany) are four phrases. The best *wazifa* you can do are four phrases. What did he say? That *Subhanallah, walhamdulillah, wa la ilaha illallah, wallahu akbar* (Glory be to Allah, and all praise is for Allah, and there is no god but Allah, and Allah is the greatest). *Allahu Akbar* comes in this. *Wa la hawla wa la quwwata illa billahil 'aliyyil 'azim* (And there is no might and no power except with Allah, the Most High, the Most Great). This fifth thing is also included. So, and it is also said in a *riwayat* (narration) that the Prophet says that a person should, before sleeping, recite this four times. That by reciting it, the benefit he gets is that he obtains the reward of one *Hajj*. So these are also such blessings. From *Subhanallah* and… *Subhanallah* indicates, as we said in the *tasbih* (rosary), *Allahu Akbar*, *Alhamdulillah*, and *Subhanallah*. So *Allahu Akbar* upon the *namiya*, *Alhamdulillah* upon the *hissiya*, and *Subhanallah* upon the *natiqa*. And the final phrase, *La ilaha illallah*, its form is upon the *nayyirah* (luminous form). Which, having united, these three souls will unite at the time of his death and they will transform into the noble form and the form of the *nayyirah*.

You said that the *hissiya* leaves its *jism* (body), it escapes. Escapes means it… ascends? No, it doesn't ascend. The *natiqa* will absorb the *hissiya*, the *namiya* it will absorb. The *hissiya* spreading into the body is a very bad death, a bad state. But the *hissiya* being absorbed into the *natiqa* is a very noble thing. The *hissiya* becomes part of the *natiqa*.

At the time of death? But during life too, it keeps pulling it, right? Yes, absolutely. Throughout life, this process keeps going on. The *natiqa* keeps absorbing it, the *hissiya*. The more obedient it is, the *hissiya*, it will absorb it.

This is why it is said that when the *awliya' Allah* (friends of God) pray, their state changes. The person who he was, that person he was not after. There are examples of this as well. That it was said that the Messenger who, on the night of *Mi'raj*, went to meet Allah, and he met with Allah, and whatever excellences you obtained, the nearness to God you obtained, then when you returned, was it that the same one who went, the same person returned? So this is very far-fetched from reason, this matter, that the one whom the Lord of the worlds summoned for a meeting, he would be sent back in the same state.

This is also, look, Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd. So, your state was such that Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman says that if it had not been decided in the *sabiq* (pre-eternity) that I would precede Ibrahim, then I could never have preceded Ibrahim. How great a statement he made. And that Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd, he remained in Yemen and you remained in Hindustan. Because his successor cannot leave Yemen. Meaning, you, there is a principle of the *da'wah* (mission) that a *Da'i* (missionary) cannot leave the island of Yemen unless his successor is present there. So Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd remained in Yemen. But in Yemen, it so happened that at that time, the Zaidi Imam who was there, he imprisoned him. He remained in jail for 14 years. Sayyidna Ibrahim bin Muhammad bin Fahd. And in those 14 years, Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman performed approximately 11 *nusus* (appointments) on him. He performed 11 *nusus*. So he was asked, that from among the *hudud* (ranks) of the *da'wah*, someone asked Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman, saying, "Master, you have already performed *nass* (appointment) on him, Sayyidna Ibrahim, then why was there a need to perform *nass* again?" So you said, "The Ibrahim upon whom I had performed *nass*, that Ibrahim has changed. Ibrahim is no longer Ibrahim." You see the implication, how great a statement was made. That that Ibrahim is no longer Ibrahim, he has changed. I performed *nass* on a different Ibrahim. This is what he said.

There must be a big age difference. Sayyidna 'Ali and Sayyidna Ibrahim. I don't know. He was the son of Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd, but their mother was the same. Khadija bint Nuh. Perhaps he married after the passing of Sayyidna Sulayman, so the age difference may not be that great.

*Wal-qatlu fi jami'i halatiha wa la taqtassu laha al-'inayat al-ilahiyya bis-su'ud*. And the divine providence did not take *qisas* (retribution) for it from its *su'ud* (ascension), that is, the *zabihat* (slaughtered animal). *Li kawnihim min azdad al-hudud* (Because of its being from the opposites of the ranks). Because it was from the opposites of the ranks. Who? Its *hissiya*. The meat of the slaughter did ascend. The meat of the slaughter did ascend and it also came into the food of the believers. But its *hissiya*, because it was from the opposites of the ranks, so its *hissiya*… from it, retribution was not taken that it should be elevated. *Li kawniha min azdad al-hudud*. *Wa yufariquhu bi wasitat al-tasmiyat al-mu'azzamah 'inda arbab al-huda* (And it separates from it by means of the magnificent naming [of God] in the view of the masters of guidance). However, it is that it separates… what thing? The *hissiya* separates, by means of the *tasmiyah*. The *tasmiyah* means by saying *Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim*. By saying *Bismillah*, it… such a *Bismillah* which is magnificent in the view of the masters of guidance, from it, it separates.

So it goes to desolate deserts. It goes where? No, what happens to it, the *hissiya* will go… Yes, it goes to the desolate deserts and *Ras al-Zanab*, that is its abode. The abode of the *hissiya* is there. And they, in their… the *hissiyat* that are there, whether they are *azdad* (opposites) or such slaughtered ones, they, what do they do? Because they are evil, so they also cause harm to the believers. And for 40 days, especially the *azdad* of the *awliya' Allah* (friends of God), they are given a respite for 40 days. They roam between the heavens and the earth. And they harm the believers. And after 40 days, they are pulled into *Ras al-Zanab*. And their abode is in *Ras al-Zanab*.

And their powers are also greatly increased. Yes. Their powers are increased. Because of this, that they had also done good deeds, no? If he is an opponent of the *da'wah*, so he has also done good deeds in his life. So the reward for those good deeds, he will not get in the hereafter. Rather, in this very world, he is given the reward that, do as you please, do whatever you want. This is the reward he is given.

But for the *zabihat*, the meat is called… *yawm an-na* it is in human form, it is ascending. But you are eating, I am eating, we are eating, it's all separate. So that which is its unit, the goat's, which is, say, Mr. X's. So that is… in 50 people it is being eaten, so all are separate. How does nature keep its account? Yes, that is… only it knows, only God knows. But it came into the state of ascending. It has come into the state of ascending. Even if it is divided, like it's with them, it's with them. Yes, yes.

Look, there is so much importance of *Bismillah* that the Prophet, in one of his prayers, says, *Bismillahilladhi la yadurru ma'a ismihi shay'un fil-ardi wa la fis-sama'i wa huwa as-sami'ul 'alim* (In the name of Allah, with whose name nothing in the earth or the heaven can cause harm, and He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing). That *Bismillah* has such power and strength that no thing can harm it, in the earth nor in the sky. And He is the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing. And this prayer is so proven, and it is also with the common people, this prayer. And Sayyidna Muhammad bin Fahd has also mentioned this prayer in his prayers. So this is such a magnificent prayer that there is also a mention somewhere of reciting it three times a day and three times at night. That by reciting this three times in the morning and at night, and from reciting this, no thing can cause harm to a person. And it is a promise of Allah and it is a promise from the tongue of the Messenger.

This matter of the *zabihat* is, say, that which we make *halal*. That matter is understood. Say, the animals that are not *halal*, their matter then goes into the *darkat* (lower levels)? Yes, absolutely, it goes into the *darkat*. Meaning, how absolutely subtle a matter this is in the world. Yes, yes. How absolutely subtle. The greatness of *halal*... the greatness of *halal* being made is a great virtue, that one *Allahu Akbar* makes a creature *halal*.

Here we who eat meat, etc., that comes from outside… A lot of caution must be taken. How it is…

The 'aqiqah of Eid al-Adha, the meat of the *zabihat* is not given to a non-Muslim. No, we usually don't give it. Yes. The one who has the *ujrat* (wage)… No, no, they don't give it. The meat of the *fiqah* (religious offering) that is there… Say, the one we had a meal of the day before yesterday… The way that meat is… one doesn't understand. No, no, they don't give it to us, but the people who take the *zabihat* of Eid al-Adha to their own homes, and they distribute it, they… God knows. It is their responsibility. But the thing is that the meat of the *zabihat*… that is, the *zabi'ah* of Eid, its meat is not given to a non-Muslim. We usually don't give it. People who are on wages, they give it to them. Then it is a wage. But otherwise, the normal meat of the *'aqiqah*, that is… No, it should not be given. And the meat of the *'aqiqah*, the mother whose child's *'aqiqah* has been performed, she should not eat it either.

Oh, so she shouldn't eat it either? No, no, she should not eat. The child is a nursing infant, no? So, if the mother eats it, in her body, that *'aqiqah* will come, and from that very meat's nutrition, the mother's strength will be formed and her milk will be formed. And the child will drink the milk, so for him, that milk is not *halal*. That milk is not *halal* for the child. This matter is generally not known to people. No, it is not known. Yes, yes. So, she should not eat it. The mother should not eat it. Okay, if, say, soup is made, should she not drink the soup either? She should not drink it. The mother should not drink it.

Look, within *halal*… how many aspects there are. Look, one animal, if we brought it, and we brought two animals, for instance. And one animal remained alive, but the second animal, due to the cold, shivered and died. So for us, it is… not… it is a dead animal, it should just be thrown away. And the one that is alive, we, by saying *Allahu Akbar*, slaughtered it. Similarly, this… an animal that dies its own death, its death is a bad one. Except for the pigeons of the Baitullah. Except for the pigeons of the Baitullah. The pigeons of the Baitullah, if they die their own death, their death is *halal*. They are not to be slaughtered. And they are not to be eaten by slaughtering them. But their own death is *halal*. Here, it was even asked of the *du'at* (missionaries) that since there are pigeons of Baitullah and also pigeons in Madinah al-Munawwarah, so are the pigeons of Madinah al-Munawwarah like the pigeons of Baitullah? It was asked. So what did the *Da'i* Sayyidna Isma'il bin Hibatullah reply? He said, "No, the pigeons of Madinah are not like the pigeons of Baitullah, because the station of the pigeons of Baitullah is very high." Yes. This is what he said. Meaning that, look, the *fuzala* (remains) of a believer, which is buried, so the *riyat* (subtle essence) which comes out within three days, they go to the moon. They go to Jupiter, then to the moon, and then *yawm an-na* they descend as completely pure and transparent matter and form the body of the Imam. Right? But its *fuzala*, which is there, it goes into the *kawākib* (planets). Into the *burūj* (constellations), sorry. It goes into the *burūj*. And in the *burūj*, they are refined. Okay, but it happens that the things which ascend are ascended from the *fuzala*, but many things remain, like hard bones, teeth, the skull, these take a very long time to be cleansed. The soft meat, etc., ascends. But the remaining things take time. And this… these things, after being refined, they come in three forms. One is that they become *jawahir* (jewels). They become jewels, because those who are the *fuzala* of the *hudud al-da'wah*, and they have performed great services in their lives. So, in reward for their services, this is given, that even their *fuzala*… *Fallahu khayrun hafizan wa huwa arhamur rahimin* (For Allah is the best of guardians, and He is the most merciful of the merciful). Allah is the best protector, and He is the most merciful of the merciful. So no thing of the believer goes to waste. Allah protects everything. Even within the *fuzala*, the things that ascend for ascension, they ascend. But the things that remain, they, after being refined, either become jewels, which are set in the crowns of kings, or they remain in their treasuries, locked up. And the second thing that happens is that these become the pigeons, from which pigeons are formed. From which pigeons are formed, whose death is *halal* and who do not have to be slaughtered. Here, look, you, they do not even perform *bawl-o-baraz* (urination and defecation). And the animal that performs the most *bawl-o-baraz* is the pigeon. Whichever house it is in, people get annoyed. Right? It makes such a mess. But, but you see, the pigeons of Baitullah do not make a mess. And they never fly over the Baitullah either. They don't even fly over Baitullah, and the pigeons of Madinah also do not make a mess. So this nobility is obtained because they are formed from the *fuzala* of the *hudud al-da'wah*. And the third thing that is obtained is the salt which is said, that *al-milhu qabla kulli ta'amin wa ba'dahu yadfa'u sab'ina da'an ahwanuha al-judham wal-junun* (Salt before and after every meal wards off seventy diseases, the least of which are leprosy and insanity). So this is the state, in that salt's state. And where did that salt come from? It's not that salt, which we put in our food, this… which we use in other things. Rather, that salt which, after being refined, comes to our table, so that we may taste it at the beginning and the end. Nature refines it. So that salt which we taste, the salt before a meal and after a meal, it has so much blessing that it wards off our diseases. And that salt is formed from the bones of the *hudud al-da'wah*.

The wild animals that die, they die their own death. The wild ones don't have the question of being slaughtered. They die. So that means they will always remain in that state, in a fallen form? In a fallen form. But in *yawm an-na*, because their resurrection has to happen, no? Yes, it happens. The disbelievers and polytheists become believers, they become Muslims. No, no, their… for the disbelievers and polytheists, it is never… since *shirk* (polytheism) is a great wrong, and until they recite the *kalimah* of *La ilaha illallah*, their progress will not happen.

At the time of every death, the *da'wah* is presented to them? Yes. Absolutely. Islam is presented. At every death… at the time of every death, and when they change bodies (*qalib*), at that time too. The *da'wah* is presented. When they change bodies, the *da'wah* is presented. To the extent that they fall and fall and come into the body of tar (*dammar*). At that time, the *da'wah* is not presented. Just as in the *munajat* there is a prayer, no? *Wa libas al-qatiran la tulinni* (and do not make me wear the garment of tar). Meaning, don't clothe me in the garment of tar. Meaning, may it not be that I fall and fall and reach this garment. Yes.

But in today's times, we are wearing it. The nylon and whatever else that comes now. Nylon, etc. Nylon is made from this. From what petrol is, the last part of it is tar. Yes, that is correct.

…a thing made of thread and a thing made of air, of which there is neither emptiness nor fullness. Meaning, in the air, there is no thing that is empty and full. In the air, no thing is empty, rather, all things are filled. It is our eyes that are not there to see it. Yes, this is why it is said that you should not become naked. In becoming naked… the things that are in the air, they will have an effect on you. So, therefore, you should not become naked. And that which is in the air and things, they have an effect. *Wa lidhalika mathaltu 'ala qatl al-mukhalifina 'inda qiyam qayam yawm ad-din* (And for that reason, I have made an analogy for the killing of the opponents at the time of the rising of the Resurrector on the Day of Judgment). And for this reason, when the *Qayam* will rise, on the Day of the Hereafter, then these opponents will be killed. Meaning, which opponents? Those who… the *azdad* (opposites). This *hissiya* (sensory soul), what was it? It was from the opposites of the ranks. So those opposites of the ranks will be given a human garment and will come near the time of *Qiyamah* (Resurrection). And they will come dressed in a human garment, the opposites. Because this *Qayam* who is coming, their station is very high, because they are the *Qayam* of the seventh *kashf* (unveiling) and the seventh *sidr* (Lote-tree). So their station is very high. So, for them, what kind of assembly will there be? That from ancient times, those who were the opposites, who have died and been destroyed, their… them, in a physical state, will be presented before the *Qayam*. Meaning, Yazid is coming, Mu'awiyah are, Marwan bin Hakam is, and Nimrud is, Fir'awn is. All of them will be brought in a human form before the *Qayam*. In the plain of Mina. Nature will drag them. Just as it is said that just as a flock is dragged, in that way, the flock will be dragged and they will be brought. In the plain of Mina. And the *Qayam 'ala dhikrihi as-salam* will command the believers to kill them. So they will kill them, and the plain of Mina will be completely filled with their corpses. To the extent that such a stench will spread from it that the believers will say that we cannot live in this state. They will complain to the Imam. The Imam will command the wind, and that wind will blow and take them towards *safrah*. But there are four doors to that *safrah*. One door is here in Hadramaut as well. So, there are four doors, its mention Sayyidna 'Ali bin Sulayman has… mentioned in a book. So… he will take them inside. And for one *qawr-e-a'zam* (great epoch), their duration is what? They will remain for one *qawr-e-a'zam*. *Illa ma rahima rabbi* (except those on whom my Lord has mercy). Except for those upon whom my Lord has shown mercy. So what is the meaning of that? That those who had done some good deeds, so because of that, they, before one *qawr*, their release will happen. And how will the release happen? Through volcanoes. Through volcanoes, as lava, they will come out. As lava, they will come out. Just as 'Umar bin 'Abd al-'Aziz… 'Abd al-'Aziz… what was he? He was a caliph. That is, from the time of Mu'awiyah, in that line he came.

So what is its example? That 'Umar bin 'Abd al-'Aziz, who was a caliph of the Muslims, and he is from the line of Mu'awiyah, that is, from the Banu Umayyah, he was a caliph. But he, what did he do? The *Bagh-e-Fadak* which was usurped by 'Umar ibn al-Khattab, and after that it remained with the governments, in their possession, so he returned it to the Ahl al-Bayt. He did one thing. One thing he did. The second thing he did was that from 70,000 pulpits, that cursing and reviling of Sayyidna 'Ali and his progeny was done, curses were hurled. And keeping the name 'Ali was forbidden. So he stopped that. And he said that this is an injustice upon the Ahl al-Bayt, and he stopped it. These two deeds that he did, so because of that, his duration was shortened, and he, before one *qawr-e-a'zam*, was released. Through a volcano.

Yes, 'Abd al-'Aziz gate. The gate is named after 'Abd al-'Aziz. A gate in the courtyard is named 'Abd al-'Aziz. No, that 'Abd al-'Aziz is theirs, of the Saudis. Saudi.

The lava that comes out… that is a good form for them, right? That they are emerging. Yes. And that lava then, the lava then… its soil is formed. Yes, and from it then, vegetation grows. And from the vegetation then, food comes. And it ascends. Ascends and refines. Yes.

And *Jabal 'Arafat* (Mount 'Arafat)…

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. O Allah, convey this Fatihah, a gift, a means, a noble, pleasing sacrifice to the soul of Sayyidna 'Ali bin Hibatullah, may Allah sanctify his soul and illuminate his grave, and may He shower upon us from his blessings, his effulgence, and his support, by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad, and accept it from us, O Allah, for the purpose of benefiting and gaining benefit in the best way possible, by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad al-Mustafa, and the great *Qaim*, and his pure and holy Ahl al-Bayt, may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon them all. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm ad-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina as-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi 'alayhim wa la ad-dallin. Amin.

By the intention of Sayyidna, our master, the master of the Husayni *da'wah* from the Sindhi lineage, the noble of nobles, the exalted and magnificent, the wise and learned, the guiding and rightly-guided, the human, the holy, Sayyidna Husayn bin Sayyidna Muhammad, Muqaddas, and his father, Sayyidna Muhammad bin Hibatullah, Muqaddas, may Allah sanctify their souls. May Allah the Exalted protect him, guard him, and aid him against the plots of the polytheists. [Arabic prayer]... by the right of the master of the messengers, Sayyidna Muhammad al-Mustafa, and his pure and holy Ahl al-Bayt, may the blessings and peace of Allah be upon them all. Al-Fatihah. Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim. Al-hamdu lillahi rabb al-'alamin. Ar-rahman ar-rahim. Maliki yawm ad-din. Iyyaka na'budu wa iyyaka nasta'in. Ihdina as-sirat al-mustaqim. Sirat alladhina an'amta 'alayhim ghayril maghdubi 'alayhim wa la ad-dallin. Amin.

Thank you. Thank you.

As-salamu alaykum.

<!-- lecture 12 -->

26, 26. As-salamu 'alaykum wa rahmatullah wa barakatuhu. These are two editions. I have page 57, and what you all have is 26, 26, 26 is the page number. Alright, I will read. *Wa amma Jabal 'Arafat*.

Bismillah al-Rahman al-Rahim (In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful). Allahumma salli 'ala Muhammad wa aali Muhammad (O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad). Wa amma Jabal 'Arafat al-sharif (And as for the noble Mount 'Arafat), fa-tahtahu sirrun latifun (beneath it is a subtle secret), wa fi Yawm 'Arafah (and on the Day of 'Arafah), tashruqu fi dhalika al-mahall (there shines in that place), min al-suwar (from the forms/images), ma la yakhturu 'ala qalbi basharin (what has never occurred to the heart of any human). Yes. And as for the matter of Jabal 'Arafat, which is noble... Jabal 'Arafat, meaning the Maqam (station) of 'Arafat, the Maidan (plain) of 'Arafat, it is necessary to set foot there for the Haji (pilgrim).
